# Helium v1 Implementation Plan

> **PHASE 3 COMPLETE — merged to master as PR #3 (merge commit c3eaeaa), deployed as v0.1.5.**
>
> **Measured acceptance results** (recorded numbers, not booleans):
>
> | AC | Criterion | Bound | Measured |
> |----|-----------|-------|----------|
> | #3 | trigger event to delivered email | 600 s | **29.2 s** |
> | #6 | rollback to the previous release | 60 s | **13 s** (14.2 s laptop wall clock) |

> operation on the mini. Start: **2026-08-25** (first full trading day after
> v0.1.5; v0.1.5 deployed 2026-08-24 11:09 ET, mid-session). Window: Tue 08-25,
> Wed 08-26, Thu 08-27, Fri 08-28, Mon 08-31.
>
> Daily check (no changes to the mini during the window):
>
> ```bash
> ssh macmini 'for d in $(seq 0 4); do f=/Users/moremeds/.helium/state/jsonl/heartbeat-$(date -u -v-${d}d +%F).jsonl; echo "$f $(wc -l < "$f" 2>/dev/null || echo MISSING)"; done'
> ```
>
> Pass when: heartbeat rows are continuous on all five days, at least one senior
> analysis email was delivered end-to-end, and no dead-man alert fired outside a

>
> Checkbox state below is superseded by the SDD ledger at
> `.superpowers/sdd/2026-08-23-helium-v1/progress.md`, which records what was
> actually executed, every deviation, and the twelve production bugs the mini
> and CI exposed. The boxes were not back-ticked wholesale: the ledger is the
> record, and re-marking ~200 boxes would add no reader-visible truth.

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Build helium v1 — an always-on reactive agent harness (dsh plugin mode) on the Mac mini that watches argon macro state, triages changes with deepseek-v4-flash, escalates material events to Claude Max, and delivers analysis via JSONL + email.

**Architecture:** One long-lived `dsh --profile helium --port 3080 --no-open` process under launchd (web UI via the `@deepseek-ai/dsh-web-app` bundle in the profile). A single deployable cordis plugin package (`dsh-plugin-helium`) wires four internal cordis plugins (toolkit / sensor / dispatch / delivery); a pure library package (`@helium/core`) holds all logic that does not touch dsh APIs, so unit tests never need a dsh runtime. Ecosystem tools are one implementation exposed twice: registered in-process for dsh agents, and served over MCP stdio to `claude -p` children.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript (ES2023, NodeNext ESM, strict), pnpm workspace, vitest, zod, `@deepseek-ai/dsh@0.1.1-rc.2` (exact pin), nodemailer, `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`, DuckDB Node bindings, launchd.

**Spec:** `docs/specs/2026-08-23-helium-design.md` — normative. Where this plan and the spec disagree, the spec wins; flag the conflict instead of silently choosing.

## Global Constraints

- Pin `@deepseek-ai/dsh@0.1.1-rc.2` **exactly** (no `^`/`~`). Upgrades are human-approved events (spec §9).
- Plugins install into the profile with **`file:` semantics and compiled JS** (`lib/`); `link:` breaks module resolution; TS under node_modules cannot be type-stripped (spec §9).
- Never touch the default `~/.dsh`. Runtime roots: `$DSH_HOME = <state-root>/dsh-home`, harness state `<state-root>/state/` — laptop dev state-root is `~/.helium-dev`, mini production state-root is `/Users/moremeds/.helium` (spec §3). The `HELIUM_STATE_ROOT` env var and `Config.stateRoot` point **directly at the state dir** (`<state-root>/state`); `DSH_HOME` is always set explicitly, never derived.
- Secrets never enter the repo, logs, JSONL records, prompts, or PR bodies. Env files: `~/.config/helium/claude-token.env` (exists on mini, 0600), `~/.config/helium/helium.env` (`DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`, SMTP; 0600). Never echo secret values (spec §12).
- All persisted timestamps UTC ISO-8601; schedules and calendar windows carry an explicit IANA tz, default `America/New_York` (spec §8).
- Read-only by default; every mutating tool sits behind per-job `allowMutations` + audit record. No trading writes ever (spec §6).
- The LLM is never in the tick loop: detection is pure code, seconds; analysis is minutes (spec §1).
- `claude -p` children always get: explicit `--allowedTools` allow-list, per-job `--max-turns`, job-owned cwd, `--output-format json`, proxy env injected only into the child (spec §4, §12).
- Triage output is a structured verdict `{escalate, severity, reason}` parsed from JSON — escalation is never inferred from prose (spec §4).
- ESM only, `.js` extensions in relative TS imports. Strict TypeScript, ES2023 target, NodeNext resolution.
- zod for all schemas (job YAML, tool params, verdicts).
- Node ≥ 20 LTS (verify against dsh's `engines` field at scaffold time; record the floor in root package.json `engines`).
- CI (GitHub Actions): typecheck + unit tests + mocked-mode contract tests only. CI never holds secrets and never makes network LLM calls.
- Live LLM calls in tests are opt-in only: `HELIUM_LIVE=1` (one real deepseek-v4-flash call, costs cents) / `HELIUM_LIVE_CLAUDE=1` (one real claude -p call). Never set in CI.

## Ground truth for dsh APIs

- `docs/spikes/2026-08-23-dsh-spike/` — working spike code (plugin, profile layout, packaging, Python SDK escape hatch). **Copy dsh API usage from here.**
- Never invent a dsh API. If a needed API is not demonstrated in the spike, verify it in the installed dsh source (`node_modules/@deepseek-ai/*`) before using it; if it cannot be verified, stop the task and record the gap in the plan.
- dsh CLI shapes proven in the spike: `dsh --profile <name>` (run), `dsh plugin add file:<abs-path>` (install into profile; **never** `link:`), profile tree at `$DSH_HOME/profiles/<name>/{cordis.yml, cordis.patch.yml, package.json}`.

```
helium/
├── package.json                 # pnpm workspace root; engines; scripts (build/test/typecheck)
├── pnpm-workspace.yaml

├── .github/workflows/ci.yml    # typecheck + unit + mocked contracts
├── .gitignore
├── docs/
│   ├── specs/2026-08-23-helium-design.md          # (exists)

│   └── superpowers/plans/2026-08-23-helium-v1.md  # this plan
├── packages/core/               # @helium/core — pure lib, zero dsh imports
│   └── src/{time,jsonl,job,state,runs,verdict,theses}.ts
│   └── src/tools/{types,argon,apex,livewire,thesis,index}.ts
│   └── src/mcp/server.ts        # + bin: helium-mcp (stdio MCP server)
├── plugins/helium/              # dsh-plugin-helium — the single deployable cordis plugin
│   └── src/{index,toolkit,sensor,dispatch,delivery,claude}.ts
│   └── cordis.patch.yml         # bundle patch (config from env, spike format)
├── contracts/                   # @helium/contracts — dsh contract suite (mocked + live)
├── profile/                     # dsh profile template → deployed into $DSH_HOME/profiles/helium
│   └── {cordis.yml, cordis.patch.yml, package.json}
├── jobs/macro-watch.yaml        # (+ jobs/apex-health.yaml in the AC#7 drill)
├── calendars/us-macro.yaml      # FOMC/CPI/NFP windows, ET-anchored
├── context/ecosystem.md         # layer-1 context injected into every agent
├── scripts/
│   ├── deploy-profile.sh        # build → pack → file:-add into a $DSH_HOME
│   ├── prereq-check.sh          # mini prerequisites (node, claude, clash, pmset, token file)
│   ├── release/{cut.sh, deploy.sh, rollback.sh}
│   ├── canary/{run.sh, diff-intel.md}
│   └── deadman/check-heartbeat.sh
└── launchd/
    ├── com.helium.dsh.plist.template
    └── com.helium.deadman.plist.template
```

Design note (recorded deviation from the spec's four-plugin diagram): the four
plugin responsibilities in spec §3 are implemented as four cordis plugins inside
**one** npm package (`dsh-plugin-helium`) wired by an umbrella `apply()`. This
keeps the §3 boundaries (each module is its own cordis plugin with its own ctx
scope) while making `deploy-profile.sh` a single `file:` install — the spike
showed per-package build→remove→add is the main friction point.

## Interface Contracts (normative for every task)

Later tasks import these exact names and signatures. A task may add members,
never rename or repurpose these.

```ts
// ── packages/core/src/time.ts
export function parseDuration(s: string): number; // '30s'|'10m'|'2h' → ms; throws on junk
export function nowIso(): string; // UTC ISO-8601

// ── packages/core/src/jsonl.ts
export class JsonlWriter {
  constructor(dir: string);
  append(stream: string, record: Record<string, unknown>): void;
  // → dir/<stream>-YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl (UTC date); injects ts: nowIso() when absent
  prune(retentionDays: number): void;
}

// ── packages/core/src/job.ts  (zod; YAML durations normalized to ms on parse)
export type Severity = "noise" | "minor" | "material" | "critical";
export interface TriggerStateChange {
  kind: "state-change";
  url: string;
  fields: string[];
  intervalMs: number;
  dedupTtlMs: number;
}
export interface TriggerCalendarWindow {
  kind: "calendar-window";
  calendar: string;
  beforeMs: number;
  afterMs: number;
  intervalDuringMs: number;
}
export interface TriggerCron {
  kind: "cron";
  schedule: string;
  tz: string;
}
export type Trigger = TriggerStateChange | TriggerCalendarWindow | TriggerCron;
export interface JobSpec {
  name: string;
  enabled: boolean;
  triggers: Trigger[];
  engine: {
    triage: { engine: "deepseek"; model: string };
    senior: { engine: "claude-max" };
  };
  escalateWhen: Exclude<Severity, "noise">;
  session: "fresh";
  memory: "none" | "thesis-file";
  tools: string[];
  allowMutations: boolean;
  maxTurns: { triage: number; senior: number };
  timeoutMs: number;
  budget: { maxTriagePerHour: number; maxSeniorPerDay: number };
  delivery: {
    jsonl: true;
    email?: { to: string; subjectPrefix: string; maxPerHour: number };
  };
  prompt: string;
}
export function parseJobYaml(text: string, source: string): JobSpec; // throws with source in message
export function loadJobs(dir: string): JobSpec[]; // all *.yaml; caller filters .enabled

// ── packages/core/src/state.ts
export interface SensorState {
  baseline?: { hash: string; fields: Record<string, unknown> };
  dedup: Record<string, string>; // dedupKey → expiry ISO
  triageFires: string[]; // ISO stamps, pruned to rolling window
  seniorFires: string[];
}
export class StateStore {
  constructor(root: string); // <state-root>/state
  loadSensor(job: string): SensorState; // missing file → empty state
  saveSensor(job: string, s: SensorState): void; // atomic: tmp + rename
}

// ── packages/core/src/runs.ts
export type RunOutcome =
  "run_completed" | "run_failed" | "timed_out" | "interrupted";
export interface RunRecord {
  runId: string;
  job: string;
  tier: "triage" | "senior";
  phase: "run_started" | RunOutcome;
  ts: string;
  detail?: Record<string, unknown>;
}
export class RunLedger {
  constructor(writer: JsonlWriter); // stream 'runs'
  start(job: string, tier: "triage" | "senior"): string;
  finish(
    runId: string,
    job: string,
    tier: "triage" | "senior",
    outcome: RunOutcome,
    detail?: Record<string, unknown>,
  ): void;
  reconcileStartup(): number; // dangling run_started (today+yesterday files) → append 'interrupted'; returns count
}

// ── packages/core/src/verdict.ts
export interface TriageVerdict {
  escalate: boolean;
  severity: Severity;
  reason: string;
}
export function parseVerdict(text: string): TriageVerdict | null; // last {...} JSON block; null if invalid
export function meetsThreshold(
  v: TriageVerdict,
  escalateWhen: Exclude<Severity, "noise">,
): boolean;

// ── packages/core/src/theses.ts
export class ThesisStore {
  constructor(stateRoot: string);
  read(job: string): string | null; // resolves theses/<job>/current under the state dir
  write(job: string, content: string): { path: string; diff: string };
  // size cap 64 KiB (reject beyond); writes <utc-ts>.md; re-points current; returns unified diff vs previous
}

// ── packages/core/src/tools/types.ts
export interface ToolRunContext {
  fetchImpl?: typeof fetch;
} // test injection
export interface EcosystemTool {
  name: string;
  description: string;
  paramsSchema: z.ZodType;
  mutating: boolean; // filtered out unless job.allowMutations
  run(args: Record<string, unknown>, ctx?: ToolRunContext): Promise<string>; // JSON string
}
export function buildTools(cfg: {
  argonBase: string;
  apexBase: string;
  livewireDb?: string;
  stateRoot: string;
}): EcosystemTool[];
// names: 'argon_api', 'apex_api', 'livewire_sql', 'thesis_read', 'thesis_write'
// thesis_* are helium-owned state (versioned, capped) — mutating: false by design

// ── packages/core/src/mcp/server.ts  (bin: helium-mcp)
// stdio MCP server exposing buildTools() filtered by env:
// HELIUM_TOOLS (csv), HELIUM_ALLOW_MUTATIONS ('1'), HELIUM_ARGON_BASE,
// HELIUM_APEX_BASE, HELIUM_LIVEWIRE_DB, HELIUM_STATE_ROOT

// ── plugins/helium (cordis; dsh API usage copied from the spike)
export interface TriggerEvent {
  job: string;
  kind: Trigger["kind"];
  firedAt: string;
  dedupKey: string;
  payload: Record<string, unknown>;
}
// sensor pure helpers (unit-testable without cordis):
export function extractFields(
  body: unknown,
  fields: string[],
): Record<string, unknown>; // dot-paths
export function hashFields(x: Record<string, unknown>): string; // sha256 hex, 12 chars
// dispatch:
export interface DispatchResult {
  runId: string;
  tier: "triage" | "senior";
  outcome: RunOutcome;
  verdict?: TriageVerdict;
  analysis?: string;
  error?: string;
}
// delivery: deliver(job: JobSpec, ev: TriggerEvent, result: DispatchResult): Promise<void>; heartbeat(row: Record<string, unknown>): void
```

**Contract amendments discovered during plan writing (normative):**

- dsh-side tool registration does NOT take zod: dsh's `ctx.tools.register` /
  `defineTool` use dsh's own `ParameterSchemaSpec` DSL (verified in dsh source,
  see Task 2.7). zod stays the source schema for `@helium/core` + MCP; the dsh
  adapter hand-writes flat `ParameterSchemaSpec` objects.
- `JobSpec` gains an optional `script?: JobScriptAction` member in Task 3.6
  (canary script action). Adding members to contracts is allowed; renaming is not.
- `HELIUM_STATE_ROOT` / `Config.stateRoot` denote the state dir itself
  (`<root>/state`); `StateStore`, `ThesisStore`, `buildTools({stateRoot})` all
  receive that same value.

## Execution conventions

- **Branches:** Task 1.1 makes the bootstrap commit directly on `master` (empty
  repo has no PR base — documented exception), then creates `feat/phase1-foundation`.
  Each phase works on its own branch (`feat/phase2-core`, `feat/phase3-production`)
  and merges to `master` via PR after CI is green. Never merge with red CI.
- **Commits:** every task ends in one or more commits. No AI attribution trailers.
- **Laptop vs mini:** phases 1–2 run entirely on the laptop (argon via
  `ssh -L 18400:127.0.0.1:8400 macmini` when a real endpoint is wanted; tests
  default to local fixtures). Phase 3 touches the mini (`ssh macmini`, user
  `moremeds`, PATH needs `$HOME/.local/bin`).
- **Production probes are read-only** until Task 3.3 deploys for real.

---
## Phase 1 — Foundation & substrate proof

Stand up the `helium` pnpm workspace, land the three pure-library modules the whole
harness rests on (`time`/`jsonl`, `job`, `state`/`runs`), and make a placeholder dsh
plugin deployable into an isolated `$DSH_HOME` through a repeatable script.
Then prove the two substrate assumptions the spec flags as unverified — dsh web UI
coexisting with our plugins in one process, and MCP stdio reaching a `claude -p`
child — and record both verdicts in the spec. Phase 1 ends with one merged PR.

**Ground truth used by this phase.** dsh API facts are taken from
`docs/spikes/2026-08-23-dsh-spike/` (packaging, profile layout, `cordis.patch.yml`
`!!js` format, `ctx.effect`/`ctx.agents` usage) and, where the spike is silent, from
the dsh 0.1.1-rc.2 source clone at
`/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-chenxi-projects-OpenAlice/1bd89add-f51b-41ad-8b97-b8b70c6323d3/scratchpad/dsh-eval`
(referred to below as `$DSH_EVAL`). Every dsh claim in this phase cites the file it
came from. Never invent a dsh API: if a needed detail is in neither place, stop the
step and record the gap in this file.

**Working directory for every step:** `/Users/chenxi/projects/helium` unless a step
says otherwise. All paths below are relative to it.

  ```ts
  /**
   * Duration literals and UTC timestamps. Job YAML carries human durations
   * ('30s', '10m', '2h'); everything persisted or compared is milliseconds.
   * @module @helium/core/time
   */

  /** Milliseconds per accepted duration suffix. */
  const UNIT_MS: Record<string, number> = {
    ms: 1,
    s: 1_000,
    m: 60_000,
    h: 3_600_000,
    d: 86_400_000,
  };

  /** A whole-number count followed by one accepted suffix, and nothing else. */
  export const DURATION_PATTERN = /^(\d+)(ms|s|m|h|d)$/;

  /**
   * Parse a duration literal into milliseconds.
   * @param s - the literal, e.g. `30s`.
   * @returns the duration in milliseconds.
   * @throws when the literal is not `<integer><ms|s|m|h|d>`.
   */
  export function parseDuration(s: string): number {
    const match = DURATION_PATTERN.exec(s.trim());
    if (match === null) {
      throw new Error(
        `invalid duration ${JSON.stringify(s)} (expected <integer><ms|s|m|h|d>, e.g. 30s)`,
      );
    }
    return Number(match[1]) * UNIT_MS[match[2]];
  }

  /** The current instant as a UTC ISO-8601 timestamp. */
  export function nowIso(): string {
    return new Date().toISOString();
  }
  ```

- [ ] **Step 9: Implement `packages/core/src/jsonl.ts`.**

  ```ts
  /**
   * Append-only daily JSONL files — helium's canonical audit record (spec §8).
   * One file per stream per UTC day; rotation is the file name, retention is
   * `prune()`.
   * @module @helium/core/jsonl
   */
  import { appendFileSync, mkdirSync, readdirSync, rmSync } from "node:fs";
  import { join } from "node:path";
  import { nowIso } from "./time.js";

  /** The UTC calendar date (`YYYY-MM-DD`) an instant belongs to. */
  export function utcDate(at: Date = new Date()): string {
    return at.toISOString().slice(0, 10);
  }

  /** The file one stream's records for a UTC day land in. */
  export function jsonlFileName(stream: string, at: Date = new Date()): string {
    return `${stream}-${utcDate(at)}.jsonl`;
  }

  /** Files `prune()` is allowed to consider: exactly the ones this writer names. */
  const DATED_FILE = /^.+-(\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2})\.jsonl$/;

  /** Milliseconds in one day. */
  const DAY_MS = 86_400_000;

  /** Writes append-only daily JSONL streams under one directory. */
  export class JsonlWriter {
    /** The directory the daily files live in. */
    readonly dir: string;

    constructor(dir: string) {
      this.dir = dir;
      mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
    }

    /**
     * Append one record to `<stream>-<UTC date>.jsonl`.
     * @param stream - the stream name, e.g. `runs`.
     * @param record - the record; a `ts` it already carries is preserved.
     */
    append(stream: string, record: Record<string, unknown>): void {
      const line = JSON.stringify({ ts: nowIso(), ...record });
      appendFileSync(join(this.dir, jsonlFileName(stream)), `${line}\n`);
    }

    /**
     * Delete date-stamped stream files older than the retention window.
     * Files this writer did not name are never touched.
     * @param retentionDays - days of history to keep.
     */
    prune(retentionDays: number): void {
      const cutoff = Date.now() - retentionDays * DAY_MS;
      for (const entry of readdirSync(this.dir)) {
        const match = DATED_FILE.exec(entry);
        if (match === null) continue;
        if (Date.parse(`${match[1]}T00:00:00.000Z`) >= cutoff) continue;
        rmSync(join(this.dir, entry));
      }
    }
  }
  ```

### Task 1.3: `@helium/core` — job schema

**Files:**

- Create: `packages/core/src/job.ts`
- Modify: `packages/core/package.json` (add `yaml`, `zod` dependencies)
- Test: `packages/core/tests/job.spec.ts`

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: `parseDuration(s: string): number` from `./time.js`.
- Produces (normative): `Severity`, `TriggerStateChange`, `TriggerCalendarWindow`, `TriggerCron`, `Trigger`, `JobSpec`, `parseJobYaml(text: string, source: string): JobSpec`, `loadJobs(dir: string): JobSpec[]` — exactly the shapes in the plan skeleton's Interface Contracts.

**Library choice.** YAML parsing uses the `yaml` npm package (eemeli/yaml): pure ESM, actively maintained, and the only mainstream parser that round-trips YAML 1.2 without a `Schema`/`load` split. `js-yaml` was rejected because its ESM story is a CJS interop wrapper. Schemas use `zod` per the plan's global constraints.

  import { mkdtempSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
  import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
  import { join } from "node:path";
  import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
  import { loadJobs, parseJobYaml } from "../src/job.js";

  /** A macro-watch-shaped job file, matching spec §5's example. */
  const MACRO_WATCH = `
  name: macro-watch
  enabled: true
  triggers:
    - kind: state-change
      url: http://127.0.0.1:8400/api/rates/snapshot
      fields: [regime.state, direction, confidence]
      interval: 30s
    - kind: calendar-window
      calendar: us-macro
      window: { before: 30m, after: 2h }
      interval_during: 10s
    - kind: cron
      schedule: "0 17 * * 1-5"
      tz: America/New_York
  engine:
    triage: { engine: deepseek, model: deepseek-v4-flash }
    senior: { engine: claude-max }
  escalate_when: severity >= material
  session: fresh
  memory: thesis-file
  tools: [argon_api, livewire_sql]
  allowMutations: false
  max_turns: { triage: 2, senior: 8 }
  timeout: 10m
  budget: { max_triage_per_hour: 30, max_senior_per_day: 12 }
  delivery:
    jsonl: true
    email: { to: operator, subject_prefix: "[helium/macro]", max_per_hour: 4 }
  prompt: |
    Analyze the change.
  `;

  describe("parseJobYaml", () => {
    it("parses a macro-watch-shaped job and normalizes durations to ms", () => {
      const job = parseJobYaml(MACRO_WATCH, "jobs/macro-watch.yaml");
      expect(job.name).toBe("macro-watch");
      expect(job.enabled).toBe(true);
      expect(job.escalateWhen).toBe("material");
      expect(job.session).toBe("fresh");
      expect(job.memory).toBe("thesis-file");
      expect(job.tools).toEqual(["argon_api", "livewire_sql"]);
      expect(job.allowMutations).toBe(false);
      expect(job.maxTurns).toEqual({ triage: 2, senior: 8 });
      expect(job.timeoutMs).toBe(600_000);
      expect(job.budget).toEqual({ maxTriagePerHour: 30, maxSeniorPerDay: 12 });
      expect(job.delivery).toEqual({
        jsonl: true,
        email: {
          to: "operator",
          subjectPrefix: "[helium/macro]",
          maxPerHour: 4,
        },
      });
      expect(job.engine).toEqual({
        triage: { engine: "deepseek", model: "deepseek-v4-flash" },
        senior: { engine: "claude-max" },
      });
      expect(job.triggers).toEqual([
        {
          kind: "state-change",
          url: "http://127.0.0.1:8400/api/rates/snapshot",
          fields: ["regime.state", "direction", "confidence"],
          intervalMs: 30_000,
          dedupTtlMs: 21_600_000,
        },
        {
          kind: "calendar-window",
          calendar: "us-macro",
          beforeMs: 1_800_000,
          afterMs: 7_200_000,
          intervalDuringMs: 10_000,
        },
        { kind: "cron", schedule: "0 17 * * 1-5", tz: "America/New_York" },
      ]);
    });

    it("honours an explicit dedup TTL", () => {
      const job = parseJobYaml(
        MACRO_WATCH.replace("interval: 30s", "interval: 30s\n      dedup: 45m"),
        "j.yaml",
      );
      expect(job.triggers[0]).toMatchObject({ dedupTtlMs: 2_700_000 });
    });

    it("rejects unknown keys", () => {
      expect(() =>
        parseJobYaml(`${MACRO_WATCH}\nmystery: 1\n`, "jobs/bad.yaml"),
      ).toThrow(/jobs\/bad\.yaml/);
      expect(() =>
        parseJobYaml(`${MACRO_WATCH}\nmystery: 1\n`, "jobs/bad.yaml"),
      ).toThrow(/[Uu]nrecognized|[Uu]nknown/);
    });

    it("names the source file when a duration is junk", () => {
      expect(() =>
        parseJobYaml(
          MACRO_WATCH.replace("interval: 30s", "interval: soon"),
          "jobs/macro-watch.yaml",
        ),
      ).toThrow(/jobs\/macro-watch\.yaml.*invalid duration/s);
    });

    it("rejects an escalate_when that is not `severity >= <severity>`", () => {
      expect(() =>
        parseJobYaml(
          MACRO_WATCH.replace("severity >= material", "when it matters"),
          "j.yaml",
        ),
      ).toThrow(/escalate_when/);
      expect(() =>
        parseJobYaml(
          MACRO_WATCH.replace("severity >= material", "severity >= noise"),
          "j.yaml",
        ),
      ).toThrow(/escalate_when/);
    });
  });

  describe("loadJobs", () => {
    it("loads every *.yaml in the directory, disabled jobs included", () => {
      const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "helium-jobs-"));
      writeFileSync(join(dir, "a-macro.yaml"), MACRO_WATCH);
      writeFileSync(
        join(dir, "b-off.yaml"),
        MACRO_WATCH.replace("enabled: true", "enabled: false").replace(
          "name: macro-watch",
          "name: off-job",
        ),
      );
      writeFileSync(join(dir, "notes.md"), "# not a job");
      const jobs = loadJobs(dir);
      expect(jobs.map((job) => [job.name, job.enabled])).toEqual([
        ["macro-watch", true],
        ["off-job", false],
      ]);
    });
  });
  ```

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement `packages/core/src/job.ts`.**

  ```ts
  /**
   * Job files (spec §5): one git-diffable YAML per job. YAML carries human
   * durations and snake_case; `JobSpec` carries milliseconds and camelCase, so
   * nothing downstream re-parses a duration.
   * @module @helium/core/job
   */
  import { readFileSync, readdirSync } from "node:fs";
  import { join } from "node:path";
  import { parse as parseYaml } from "yaml";
  import { z } from "zod";
  import { DURATION_PATTERN, parseDuration } from "./time.js";

  export type Severity = "noise" | "minor" | "material" | "critical";

  export interface TriggerStateChange {
    kind: "state-change";
    url: string;
    fields: string[];
    intervalMs: number;
    dedupTtlMs: number;
  }

  export interface TriggerCalendarWindow {
    kind: "calendar-window";
    calendar: string;
    beforeMs: number;
    afterMs: number;
    intervalDuringMs: number;
  }

  export interface TriggerCron {
    kind: "cron";
    schedule: string;
    tz: string;
  }

  export type Trigger =
    TriggerStateChange | TriggerCalendarWindow | TriggerCron;

  export interface JobSpec {
    name: string;
    enabled: boolean;
    triggers: Trigger[];
    engine: {
      triage: { engine: "deepseek"; model: string };
      senior: { engine: "claude-max" };
    };
    escalateWhen: Exclude<Severity, "noise">;
    session: "fresh";
    memory: "none" | "thesis-file";
    tools: string[];
    allowMutations: boolean;
    maxTurns: { triage: number; senior: number };
    timeoutMs: number;
    budget: { maxTriagePerHour: number; maxSeniorPerDay: number };
    delivery: {
      jsonl: true;
      email?: { to: string; subjectPrefix: string; maxPerHour: number };
    };
    prompt: string;
  }

  /** Spec §8: dedup carries an explicit key and TTL; 6h when the file omits it. */
  const DEFAULT_DEDUP_TTL_MS = parseDuration("6h");

  /** Spec §8: schedules and windows carry an explicit IANA tz. */
  const DEFAULT_TZ = "America/New_York";

  /** A YAML duration literal, normalized to milliseconds. */
  const duration = z
    .string()
    .refine(
      (value) => DURATION_PATTERN.test(value.trim()),
      "invalid duration (expected <integer><ms|s|m|h|d>, e.g. 30s)",
    )
    .transform((value) => parseDuration(value));

  /** `escalate_when: severity >= material` — the only accepted form (spec §4). */
  const escalateWhen = z
    .string()
    .refine(
      (value) =>
        /^severity\s*>=\s*(minor|material|critical)$/.test(value.trim()),
      "escalate_when must read 'severity >= <minor|material|critical>'",
    )
    .transform(
      (value) =>
        /(minor|material|critical)$/.exec(value.trim())![1] as Exclude<
          Severity,
          "noise"
        >,
    );

  const stateChangeYaml = z
    .object({
      kind: z.literal("state-change"),
      url: z.string().min(1),
      fields: z.array(z.string()).min(1),
      interval: duration,
      dedup: duration.optional(),
    })
    .strict();

  const calendarWindowYaml = z
    .object({
      kind: z.literal("calendar-window"),
      calendar: z.string().min(1),
      window: z.object({ before: duration, after: duration }).strict(),
      interval_during: duration,
    })
    .strict();

  const cronYaml = z
    .object({
      kind: z.literal("cron"),
      schedule: z.string().min(1),
      tz: z.string().min(1).optional(),
    })
    .strict();

  type TriggerYaml =
    | z.infer<typeof stateChangeYaml>
    | z.infer<typeof calendarWindowYaml>
    | z.infer<typeof cronYaml>;

  /** Normalize one parsed YAML trigger into its `Trigger` shape. */
  function toTrigger(raw: TriggerYaml): Trigger {
    switch (raw.kind) {
      case "state-change":
        return {
          kind: "state-change",
          url: raw.url,
          fields: raw.fields,
          intervalMs: raw.interval,
          dedupTtlMs: raw.dedup ?? DEFAULT_DEDUP_TTL_MS,
        };
      case "calendar-window":
        return {
          kind: "calendar-window",
          calendar: raw.calendar,
          beforeMs: raw.window.before,
          afterMs: raw.window.after,
          intervalDuringMs: raw.interval_during,
        };
      case "cron":
        return {
          kind: "cron",
          schedule: raw.schedule,
          tz: raw.tz ?? DEFAULT_TZ,
        };
    }
  }

  const trigger = z
    .discriminatedUnion("kind", [stateChangeYaml, calendarWindowYaml, cronYaml])
    .transform(toTrigger);

  const jobYaml = z
    .object({
      name: z.string().min(1),
      enabled: z.boolean().optional(),
      triggers: z.array(trigger).min(1),
      engine: z
        .object({
          triage: z
            .object({ engine: z.literal("deepseek"), model: z.string().min(1) })
            .strict(),
          senior: z.object({ engine: z.literal("claude-max") }).strict(),
        })
        .strict(),
      escalate_when: escalateWhen,
      session: z.literal("fresh"),
      memory: z.enum(["none", "thesis-file"]),
      tools: z.array(z.string()),
      allowMutations: z.boolean().optional(),
      max_turns: z
        .object({
          triage: z.number().int().positive(),
          senior: z.number().int().positive(),
        })
        .strict(),
      timeout: duration,
      budget: z
        .object({
          max_triage_per_hour: z.number().int().nonnegative(),
          max_senior_per_day: z.number().int().nonnegative(),
        })
        .strict(),
      delivery: z
        .object({
          jsonl: z.literal(true),
          email: z
            .object({
              to: z.string().min(1),
              subject_prefix: z.string(),
              max_per_hour: z.number().int().nonnegative(),
            })
            .strict()
            .optional(),
        })
        .strict(),
      prompt: z.string().min(1),
    })
    .strict();

  /** Rename the YAML surface to the internal `JobSpec` surface. */
  function toJobSpec(raw: z.infer<typeof jobYaml>): JobSpec {
    return {
      name: raw.name,
      enabled: raw.enabled ?? true,
      triggers: raw.triggers,
      engine: raw.engine,
      escalateWhen: raw.escalate_when,
      session: raw.session,
      memory: raw.memory,
      tools: raw.tools,
      allowMutations: raw.allowMutations ?? false,
      maxTurns: { triage: raw.max_turns.triage, senior: raw.max_turns.senior },
      timeoutMs: raw.timeout,
      budget: {
        maxTriagePerHour: raw.budget.max_triage_per_hour,
        maxSeniorPerDay: raw.budget.max_senior_per_day,
      },
      delivery: {
        jsonl: true,
        ...(raw.delivery.email === undefined
          ? {}
          : {
              email: {
                to: raw.delivery.email.to,
                subjectPrefix: raw.delivery.email.subject_prefix,
                maxPerHour: raw.delivery.email.max_per_hour,
              },
            }),
      },
      prompt: raw.prompt,
    };
  }

  /**
   * Parse one job file.
   * @param text - the YAML text.
   * @param source - the file name, included in every thrown message.
   * @returns the normalized job.
   * @throws when the YAML is malformed or violates the job schema.
   */
  export function parseJobYaml(text: string, source: string): JobSpec {
    let raw: unknown;
    try {
      raw = parseYaml(text);
    } catch (error) {
      throw new Error(`${source}: invalid YAML — ${(error as Error).message}`);
    }
    const result = jobYaml.safeParse(raw);
    if (!result.success) {
      const detail = result.error.issues
        .map(
          (issue) =>
            `${issue.path.join(".") === "" ? "<root>" : issue.path.join(".")}: ${issue.message}`,
        )
        .join("; ");
      throw new Error(`${source}: ${detail}`);
    }
    return toJobSpec(result.data);
  }

  /**
   * Load every `*.yaml` job in a directory, in file-name order.
   * Disabled jobs are returned too; callers filter on `.enabled`.
   * @param dir - the jobs directory.
   * @returns the parsed jobs.
   */
  export function loadJobs(dir: string): JobSpec[] {
    return readdirSync(dir)
      .filter((entry) => entry.endsWith(".yaml"))
      .sort()
      .map((entry) => {
        const path = join(dir, entry);
        return parseJobYaml(readFileSync(path, "utf8"), path);
      });
  }
  ```

  Recorded reconciliation with spec §5: the spec's example writes the calendar source as a comment (`# source: calendars/us-macro.yaml`), while the plan skeleton's `TriggerCalendarWindow` requires a `calendar: string`. This schema requires an explicit `calendar:` key in YAML — the Interface Contract wins, and the field name is the spec's own illustrative-vs-normative distinction.

  ```ts
  /**
   * Per-job sensor state (spec §8): baseline hash, dedup keys, and the rolling
   * budget fire stamps. Plain files under `<stateDir>/sensors/`.
   * @module @helium/core/state
   */
  import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
  import { mkdirSync, readFileSync, renameSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
  import { join } from "node:path";

  export interface SensorState {
    baseline?: { hash: string; fields: Record<string, unknown> };
    /** dedupKey → expiry ISO. */
    dedup: Record<string, string>;
    /** ISO stamps, pruned to the rolling budget window. */
    triageFires: string[];
    seniorFires: string[];
  }

  /** The state a job has before its first poll. */
  export function emptySensorState(): SensorState {
    return { dedup: {}, triageFires: [], seniorFires: [] };
  }

  /** Job names become file names, so they may not carry path syntax. */
  function assertJobName(job: string): void {
    if (job === "" || /[/\\]/.test(job) || job === "." || job === "..") {
      throw new Error(`invalid job name ${JSON.stringify(job)}`);
    }
  }

  /** File-backed sensor state under one state root. */
  export class StateStore {
    /** The harness state dir (HELIUM_STATE_ROOT). */
    readonly root: string;

    constructor(root: string) {
      this.root = root;
      mkdirSync(join(root, "sensors"), { recursive: true });
    }

    /** The file one job's state lives in. */
    private sensorPath(job: string): string {
      assertJobName(job);
      return join(this.root, "sensors", `${job}.json`);
    }

    /**
     * Read one job's state.
     * @param job - the job name.
     * @returns the persisted state, or an empty state when no file exists.
     */
    loadSensor(job: string): SensorState {
      let text: string;
      try {
        text = readFileSync(this.sensorPath(job), "utf8");
      } catch (error) {
        if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "ENOENT")
          return emptySensorState();
        throw error;
      }
      return { ...emptySensorState(), ...(JSON.parse(text) as SensorState) };
    }

    /**
     * Write one job's state atomically — a killed process never leaves a
     * half-written state file behind (spec §8).
     * @param job - the job name.
     * @param s - the state to persist.
     */
    saveSensor(job: string, s: SensorState): void {
      const target = this.sensorPath(job);
      const tmp = `${target}.${randomUUID()}.tmp`;
      writeFileSync(tmp, `${JSON.stringify(s, undefined, 2)}\n`);
      renameSync(tmp, target);
    }
  }
  ```

            runId: "r-done",
            job: "macro-watch",
            tier: "triage",
            phase: "run_completed",

      const ledger = new RunLedger(new JsonlWriter(dir));

    });
  });

- [ ] **Step 8: Implement `packages/core/src/runs.ts`.**

  ```ts
  /**
   * Two-phase run accounting (spec §8): `run_started` then exactly one outcome.
   * Startup reconciliation turns rows left dangling by a kill or a sleep into
   * `interrupted`, which is what makes the §13 kill-test mechanically possible.
   * @module @helium/core/runs
   */
  import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
  import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
  import { join } from "node:path";
  import { JsonlWriter, jsonlFileName } from "./jsonl.js";

  export type RunOutcome =
    "run_completed" | "run_failed" | "timed_out" | "interrupted";

  export interface RunRecord {
    runId: string;
    job: string;
    tier: "triage" | "senior";
    phase: "run_started" | RunOutcome;
    ts: string;
    detail?: Record<string, unknown>;
  }

  /** The stream every run row lands in. */
  const RUNS_STREAM = "runs";

  /** Phases that close a run. `interrupted` closes it too, which is what makes reconcile idempotent. */
  const TERMINAL: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set<RunOutcome>([
    "run_completed",
    "run_failed",
    "timed_out",
    "interrupted",
  ]);

  /** Milliseconds in one day. */
  const DAY_MS = 86_400_000;

  /** Appends run rows to the `runs` JSONL stream and reconciles them at startup. */
  export class RunLedger {
    private readonly writer: JsonlWriter;

    constructor(writer: JsonlWriter) {
      this.writer = writer;
    }

    /**
     * Open a run.
     * @param job - the job name.
     * @param tier - which lane is running.
     * @returns the new run id.
     */
    start(job: string, tier: "triage" | "senior"): string {
      const runId = randomUUID();
      this.writer.append(RUNS_STREAM, {
        runId,
        job,
        tier,
        phase: "run_started",
      });
      return runId;
    }

    /**
     * Close a run with its outcome.
     * @param runId - the id `start()` returned.
     * @param job - the job name.
     * @param tier - which lane ran.
     * @param outcome - how the run ended.
     * @param detail - optional structured context (never secrets).
     */
    finish(
      runId: string,
      job: string,
      tier: "triage" | "senior",
      outcome: RunOutcome,
      detail?: Record<string, unknown>,
    ): void {
      this.writer.append(
        RUNS_STREAM,
        detail === undefined
          ? { runId, job, tier, phase: outcome }
          : { runId, job, tier, phase: outcome, detail },
      );
    }

    /**
     * Close every run left open by a kill, a crash, or a machine sleep.
     * @returns how many runs were marked `interrupted`.
     */
    reconcileStartup(): number {
      const open = new Map<string, RunRecord>();
      for (const record of this.recentRuns()) {
        if (record.phase === "run_started") {
          open.set(record.runId, record);
          continue;
        }
        if (TERMINAL.has(record.phase)) open.delete(record.runId);
      }
      for (const record of open.values()) {
        this.writer.append(RUNS_STREAM, {
          runId: record.runId,
          job: record.job,
          tier: record.tier,
          phase: "interrupted",
          detail: { startedAt: record.ts },
        });
      }
      return open.size;
    }

    /** Every parsable run row from yesterday's and today's UTC files, in order. */
    private recentRuns(): RunRecord[] {
      const now = Date.now();
      const records: RunRecord[] = [];
      for (const at of [new Date(now - DAY_MS), new Date(now)]) {
        let text: string;
        try {
          text = readFileSync(
            join(this.writer.dir, jsonlFileName(RUNS_STREAM, at)),
            "utf8",
          );
        } catch (error) {
          if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "ENOENT") continue;
          throw error;
        }
        for (const line of text.split("\n")) {
          if (line.trim() === "") continue;
          let record: RunRecord;
          try {
            record = JSON.parse(line) as RunRecord;
          } catch {
            // A torn final line is an incomplete write, not a dangling run.
            continue;
          }
          if (
            typeof record.runId === "string" &&
            typeof record.phase === "string"
          ) {
            records.push(record);
          }
        }
      }
      return records;
    }
  }
  ```

### Task 1.5: dsh profile template, placeholder plugin, and `deploy-profile.sh`

**Files:**

- Create: `plugins/helium/package.json`, `plugins/helium/tsconfig.json`, `plugins/helium/cordis.patch.yml`, `plugins/helium/src/index.ts`
- Create: `profile/package.json`, `profile/cordis.yml`, `profile/cordis.patch.yml`
- Create: `scripts/deploy-profile.sh`
- Modify: root `package.json` (pin `@deepseek-ai/dsh`)
- Test: none (script + config — verification steps instead of TDD; the executable contracts are Task 1.6)

**Interfaces:**

- Consumes: nothing from `@helium/core` (the placeholder is deliberately empty).
- Produces:
  - npm package `dsh-plugin-helium` with `main: ./lib/index.js`, `dsh.bundle.patch: ./cordis.patch.yml`, exporting `name = 'helium'`, `interface Config { tickFile?: string }`, and `apply(ctx: Context, config: Config): void`.
  - `scripts/deploy-profile.sh --dsh-home <dir> [--plugin-dir <dir>]`, consumed by Task 1.6's contract suite and by Task 1.7's spikes.

**dsh facts this task relies on (all verified, with citations):**

- A profile directory is `$DSH_HOME/profiles/<name>` and holds `package.json` (with `dependencies` + `dsh.profile.bundles`) plus `cordis.patch.yml` — `$DSH_EVAL/packages/boot/app-boot/README.md` "Profiles", and `$DSH_EVAL/packages/boot/app-boot/src/profile.ts:39` (`PROFILE_PATCH_FILENAME = 'cordis.patch.yml'`).
- `initProfile` also writes a `pnpm-workspace.yaml` containing `packages: - .` / `nodeLinker: hoisted` / `autoInstallPeers: false` — `$DSH_EVAL/packages/boot/app-boot/src/profile.ts:136-170`. A hand-built profile that omits it resolves the plugin's peers to duplicate copies instead of the installation's, so `deploy-profile.sh` writes the same file.
- `cordis.yml` in a profile directory is **not** an input: `prepareProfile()` overwrites it at every boot with a fixed comment banner — `$DSH_EVAL/apps/cli/src/profile-boot.ts:59-62,98-103`. That banner is byte-identical to `docs/spikes/2026-08-23-dsh-spike/profile-sensor/cordis.yml`, which is why the spike has one. We ship it for readability only.
- A patch file that is empty or comments-only throws; disable a layer with `[]` — `$DSH_EVAL/packages/boot/app-boot/README.md`.
- `dsh --profile <name> --dump-config` composes and prints the tree **without booting** and without evaluating `!!js` — `$DSH_EVAL/apps/cli/src/dump-config.ts`. It rejects any app argument, so never combine it with `--help`.
- Bundle-patch format (`- insert:` with `id`/`name`/`config`, `!!js process.env.X`) is copied verbatim from `docs/spikes/2026-08-23-dsh-spike/plugin-cordis.patch.yml`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the placeholder plugin source.**
      `plugins/helium/src/index.ts`:

  ```ts
  /**
   * helium — placeholder cordis plugin. Phase 1 proves the packaging, profile,
   * and deploy loop; the real toolkit/sensor/dispatch/delivery plugins land in
   * phase 2. The optional tick timer exists only so the contract suite can
   * prove `ctx.effect` fires inside a booted profile without any LLM call.
   * @module dsh-plugin-helium
   */
  import { appendFileSync } from "node:fs";
  import type { Context } from "@deepseek-ai/cordis";

  export const name = "helium";

  export interface Config {
    /** Contract-test hook: when set, append one line every 100 ms to this path. */
    tickFile?: string;
  }

  /**
   * Mount the plugin.
   * @param ctx - the cordis context this plugin owns.
   * @param config - the row's config, composed from the profile patch layers.
   */
  export function apply(ctx: Context, config: Config): void {
    console.log("helium plugin mounted");
    const tickFile = config.tickFile;
    if (tickFile === undefined || tickFile === "") return;
    ctx.effect(() => {
      const timer = setInterval(() => {
        appendFileSync(tickFile, `${new Date().toISOString()}\n`);
      }, 100);
      return () => {
        clearInterval(timer);
      };
    }, "helium.contract-tick()");
  }
  ```

  `plugins/helium/cordis.patch.yml`:

  ```yaml
  # Bundle patch: inserts helium's row into any profile that lists this package
  # in dsh.profile.bundles. Format copied from the 2026-08-23 dsh spike.
  - insert:
      - id: helium
        name: "dsh-plugin-helium"
        config:
          tickFile: !!js process.env.HELIUM_CONTRACT_TICK_FILE
  ```

- [ ] **Step 4: Build the plugin and verify the compiled output.**

  ```bash
  pnpm -F dsh-plugin-helium build && ls plugins/helium/lib
  ```

  Expected: `index.d.ts  index.js` (and map files). If `tsc` cannot resolve `@deepseek-ai/cordis`, stop — the version pinned in Step 2 does not exist on the registry; re-read it from `node_modules/@deepseek-ai/cordis/package.json` rather than guessing.

- [ ] **Step 5: Write the profile template.**
      `profile/package.json` — `__HELIUM_PLUGIN_DIR__` is rewritten to an absolute path by the deploy script, because the path differs per machine:

  ```json
  {
    "name": "dsh-profile-helium",
    "private": true,
    "dsh": {
      "profile": {
        "bundles": ["@deepseek-ai/dsh-base", "dsh-plugin-helium"]
      }
    },
    "dependencies": {
      "dsh-plugin-helium": "file:__HELIUM_PLUGIN_DIR__"
    }
  }
  ```

  `profile/cordis.yml` — byte-identical to the spike's, and to the banner dsh rewrites at every boot:

  ```yaml
  # dsh profile root — an empty entry list. The tree is composed as patches:
  # each bundle in package.json's dsh.profile.bundles, then cordis.patch.yml, then any
  # --patch overlays. Edit cordis.patch.yml, not this file.
  []
  ```

  `profile/cordis.patch.yml` — the user layer, applied after every bundle layer; an id-targeted patch replaces the row's **whole** config, so every field helium wants must be restated here:

  ```yaml
  # helium's profile patch layer. Applied after the dsh-plugin-helium bundle
  # layer, so this block replaces that row's entire config — restate every
  # field, never only the changed one.
  - id: helium
    config:
      tickFile: !!js process.env.HELIUM_CONTRACT_TICK_FILE
  ```

- [ ] **Step 6: Write `scripts/deploy-profile.sh`.**
      The block below is indented two spaces by this document. Strip that indentation when writing the file — the `<<'YAML'` heredoc terminator must sit at column 0, and an indented `YAML` line will hang the script.

  ```bash
  #!/usr/bin/env bash
  # Build dsh-plugin-helium and install the helium profile into a $DSH_HOME.
  #
  # Spec §9.2: plugins install with `file:` semantics and compiled JS. `file:`
  # makes pnpm COPY the plugin, so the copy must be removed before reinstall or
  # a rebuilt lib/ never reaches the profile — that is the build → remove → add
  # loop the 2026-08-23 spike identified as the main friction point.
  set -euo pipefail

  REPO_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
  DSH_HOME_ARG=""
  PLUGIN_DIR="$REPO_ROOT/plugins/helium"

  while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do
    case "$1" in
      --dsh-home) DSH_HOME_ARG="${2:-}"; shift 2 ;;
      --plugin-dir) PLUGIN_DIR="${2:-}"; shift 2 ;;
      *) echo "deploy-profile: unknown argument $1" >&2; exit 2 ;;
    esac
  done

  if [ -z "$DSH_HOME_ARG" ]; then
    echo "deploy-profile: --dsh-home <dir> is required" >&2
    exit 2
  fi

  mkdir -p "$DSH_HOME_ARG"
  DSH_HOME_ABS="$(cd "$DSH_HOME_ARG" && pwd)"

  echo "deploy-profile: building dsh-plugin-helium"
  pnpm -C "$REPO_ROOT" -F dsh-plugin-helium build

  PLUGIN_ABS="$(cd "$PLUGIN_DIR" && pwd)"
  if [ ! -f "$PLUGIN_ABS/lib/index.js" ]; then
    echo "deploy-profile: $PLUGIN_ABS/lib/index.js missing after build" >&2
    exit 1
  fi

  PROFILE_DIR="$DSH_HOME_ABS/profiles/helium"
  mkdir -p "$PROFILE_DIR"

  sed "s|__HELIUM_PLUGIN_DIR__|$PLUGIN_ABS|" "$REPO_ROOT/profile/package.json" > "$PROFILE_DIR/package.json"
  cp "$REPO_ROOT/profile/cordis.yml" "$PROFILE_DIR/cordis.yml"
  cp "$REPO_ROOT/profile/cordis.patch.yml" "$PROFILE_DIR/cordis.patch.yml"

  # dsh's own initProfile writes exactly this file
  # (packages/boot/app-boot/src/profile.ts). A hand-built profile that omits it
  # gets duplicate cordis copies instead of the installation's single instance.
  cat > "$PROFILE_DIR/pnpm-workspace.yaml" <<'YAML'
  packages:
    - .

  nodeLinker: hoisted
  autoInstallPeers: false
  YAML

  rm -rf "$PROFILE_DIR/node_modules" "$PROFILE_DIR/pnpm-lock.yaml"

  echo "deploy-profile: installing profile at $PROFILE_DIR"
  pnpm -C "$PROFILE_DIR" install

  echo "deploy-profile: helium profile ready at $PROFILE_DIR"
  ```

  Then make it executable:

  ```bash
  chmod +x scripts/deploy-profile.sh
  ```

- [ ] **Step 7: Verify the deploy loop against a throwaway `$DSH_HOME`.**

  ```bash
  HELIUM_TEST_HOME="$(mktemp -d /tmp/helium-dsh-home.XXXXXX)"
  echo "$HELIUM_TEST_HOME"
  ./scripts/deploy-profile.sh --dsh-home "$HELIUM_TEST_HOME"
  ls "$HELIUM_TEST_HOME/profiles/helium"
  ls "$HELIUM_TEST_HOME/profiles/helium/node_modules/dsh-plugin-helium/lib"
  ```

  Expected: the script prints `helium profile ready at …`; the profile directory holds `cordis.patch.yml  cordis.yml  node_modules  package.json  pnpm-lock.yaml  pnpm-workspace.yaml`; and the installed plugin copy contains `index.js`. Confirm `grep 'file:' "$HELIUM_TEST_HOME/profiles/helium/package.json"` shows an absolute path with no `__HELIUM_PLUGIN_DIR__` left.

- [ ] **Step 8: Verify the profile actually composes under dsh.**

  ```bash
  DSH_HOME="$HELIUM_TEST_HOME" ./node_modules/.bin/dsh --profile helium --dump-config | tee /tmp/helium-dump.yml | head -40
  grep -c 'dsh-plugin-helium' /tmp/helium-dump.yml
  ```

  Expected: exit 0, and `grep -c` reports at least 1 — `--dump-config` prints the composed tree with one comment layer per bundle, so `dsh-plugin-helium` appears both as a layer label and as the inserted row's `name`. This is the cheapest proof that the profile mounts: it runs `prepareProfile` + `loadProfile` (the same code path a boot uses) and fails loud on a listed bundle that does not declare `dsh.bundle`, but never starts the tree (`$DSH_EVAL/apps/cli/src/dump-config.ts`). Do **not** use `dsh --profile helium --help`: after `--profile` every token belongs to the booted app, and `--dump-config` explicitly rejects app arguments (`$DSH_EVAL/apps/cli/src/args.ts`). The full boot proof is Task 1.6 Step 7.

  git add plugins profile scripts package.json pnpm-lock.yaml
  git commit -m "feat(profile): dsh-plugin-helium placeholder, helium profile template, deploy-profile.sh"
  ```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write `contracts/src/dsh.ts`.**

  ```ts
  /**
   * The dsh installation under contract. The canary (spec §10) reruns this same
   * suite against a candidate version by setting HELIUM_DSH_VERSION and
   * HELIUM_DSH_BIN at an isolated install; everything else defaults to the pin.
   * @module @helium/contracts/dsh
   */
  import { execFileSync } from "node:child_process";
  import { mkdtempSync } from "node:fs";
  import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
  import { dirname, join, resolve } from "node:path";
  import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";

  /** The dsh version helium pins (plan global constraint; spec §9.1). */
  export const PINNED_DSH_VERSION = "0.1.1-rc.2";

  /** The dsh version this run exercises. */
  export const dshVersion: string =
    process.env.HELIUM_DSH_VERSION ?? PINNED_DSH_VERSION;

  /** Repository root — contracts/src → contracts → repo. */
  export const repoRoot: string = resolve(
    dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)),
    "..",
    "..",
  );

  /** The dsh binary this run drives. */
  export const dshBin: string =
    process.env.HELIUM_DSH_BIN ?? join(repoRoot, "node_modules", ".bin", "dsh");

  /** A throwaway `$DSH_HOME` — never the operator's default `~/.dsh` (spec §9.4). */
  export function makeDshHome(): string {
    return mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "helium-dsh-home-"));
  }

  /** Install the helium profile into a throwaway home through the real deploy script. */
  export function deployHeliumProfile(dshHome: string): void {
    execFileSync(
      join(repoRoot, "scripts", "deploy-profile.sh"),
      ["--dsh-home", dshHome],
      {
        cwd: repoRoot,
        stdio: "pipe",
        encoding: "utf8",
      },
    );
  }
  ```

- [ ] **Step 6: Write the `ctx.effect` timer contract.**
      `contracts/tests/effect-timer.contract.spec.ts`:

  ```ts
  import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
  import { existsSync, readFileSync, rmSync } from "node:fs";
  import { join } from "node:path";
  import { afterAll, beforeAll, describe, expect, it } from "vitest";
  import { deployHeliumProfile, dshBin, makeDshHome } from "../src/dsh.js";

  /** Resolve once `predicate()` holds, or reject after `timeoutMs`. */
  async function waitFor(
    predicate: () => boolean,
    timeoutMs: number,
    label: string,
  ): Promise<void> {
    const deadline = Date.now() + timeoutMs;
    while (Date.now() < deadline) {
      if (predicate()) return;
      await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 100));
    }
    throw new Error(`timed out waiting for ${label}`);
  }

  describe("contract: ctx.effect interval timers run inside a booted profile", () => {
    let dshHome: string;

    beforeAll(() => {
      dshHome = makeDshHome();
      deployHeliumProfile(dshHome);
    });

    afterAll(() => {
      rmSync(dshHome, { recursive: true, force: true });
    });

    it("fires the plugin timer and stops cleanly on SIGTERM", async () => {
      const tickFile = join(dshHome, "ticks.log");
      const stderr: string[] = [];
      const stdout: string[] = [];
      const child = spawn(dshBin, ["--profile", "helium"], {
        env: {
          ...process.env,
          DSH_HOME: dshHome,
          HELIUM_CONTRACT_TICK_FILE: tickFile,
        },
        stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"],
      });
      child.stdout.on("data", (chunk) => stdout.push(String(chunk)));
      child.stderr.on("data", (chunk) => stderr.push(String(chunk)));
      const exited = new Promise<number | null>((resolve) =>
        child.on("exit", (code) => resolve(code)),
      );

      try {
        await waitFor(
          () =>
            existsSync(tickFile) &&
            readFileSync(tickFile, "utf8").trim().split("\n").length >= 2,
          60_000,
          `two ticks in ${tickFile}; stderr was:\n${stderr.join("")}`,
        );
      } finally {
        child.kill("SIGTERM");
      }

      expect(stdout.join("")).toContain("helium plugin mounted");
      expect(await exited).toBe(0);
    });
  });
  ```

  Grounding: `runProfile` "leaves process lifetime to the mounted plugins", so a plugin holding a `setInterval` keeps the process alive with no app surface mounted; `SIGTERM` is "a supervisor's ordinary stop request and exits 0 on every surface" — `$DSH_EVAL/apps/cli/src/profile-boot.ts:200-225`. The timer body is copied from the spike's `ctx.effect(() => { const timer = setInterval(...); return () => clearInterval(timer) }, label)`.

  ```bash
  pnpm run test:contracts contracts/tests/effect-timer.contract.spec.ts
  ```

  Expected: `1 passed` within roughly 10 s. If the process exits immediately instead of ticking, capture its stderr and record the finding here before changing anything — that would falsify the "plugins own process lifetime" reading.

  `contracts/fixtures/plugin-live-dispatch/cordis.patch.yml`:

  ```yaml

  `contracts/fixtures/plugin-live-dispatch/src/index.ts`:

  ```ts
  /**
   * Live contract fixture: one real agent turn through the exact seam helium
   * depends on — ctx.agents.create → followup → whenIdle → session-event
   * watermark capture. Copied from the 2026-08-23 dsh spike plugin.
   * @module dsh-plugin-helium-live-dispatch
   */
  import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
  import { appendFileSync } from "node:fs";
  import type { Context } from "@deepseek-ai/cordis";
  import type {} from "@deepseek-ai/cordis-plugin-loader";
  import {
    installModelSelection,
    type Agent,
    type ModelSelectionRef,
  } from "@deepseek-ai/dsh-agent";
  import type {} from "@deepseek-ai/dsh-agent-default-model";
  import { createUserMessage } from "@deepseek-ai/dsh-llm";
  import { SessionId, type SessionEvent } from "@deepseek-ai/dsh-session";

  export const name = "helium-live-dispatch";
  export const inject = ["agentDefaultModel", "agents", "sessions"];

  export interface Config {
    outFile: string;
  }

  /** Last non-empty assistant text in the durable interval we own. */
  function finalText(
    events: readonly SessionEvent[],
    firstSeq: number,
  ): string {
    let text = "";
    for (const event of events) {
      if (event.seq < firstSeq || event.type !== "assistant/message") continue;
      const joined = event.data.message.content
        .filter((block) => block.type === "text")
        .map((block) => block.text)
        .join("");
      if (joined !== "") text = joined;
    }
    return text;
  }

  export function apply(ctx: Context, config: Config): void {
    ctx.effect(() => {
      let cancelled = false;
      void (async (): Promise<void> => {
        await ctx.get("loader")?.await();
        const selection = ctx.agentDefaultModel.currentSelection();
        const created = await ctx.agents.create({
          sessionId: SessionId(`session-${randomUUID()}`),
          meta: { cwd: process.cwd() },
          agentOptions: {
            provider: selection.provider,
            model: selection.model,
          },
          setup: (agentCtx) => {
            const selected: ModelSelectionRef = {
              current: selection,
              assembled: undefined,
            };
            installModelSelection(agentCtx, selected);
          },
        });
        const agent: Agent = created.agent;
        await agent.whenIdle();
        if (cancelled) return;
        const firstSeq = agent.session.seq;
        const startedAt = Date.now();
        agent.followup(
          createUserMessage({
            content: [
              {
                type: "text",
                text: "Reply with exactly the word HELIUM_CONTRACT_OK and nothing else. Do not use any tools.",
              },
            ],
            source: { kind: "user" },
          }),
        );
        await agent.whenIdle();
        await ctx.sessions.flush(agent.session);
        appendFileSync(
          config.outFile,
          `${JSON.stringify({
            timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
            latencyMs: Date.now() - startedAt,
            sessionId: String(agent.id),
            finalText: finalText(agent.session.events, firstSeq),
          })}\n`,
        );
      })().catch((error: unknown) => {
        console.error("helium-live-dispatch:", error);
      });
      return () => {
        cancelled = true;
      };
    }, "helium-live-dispatch.run()");
  }
  ```

  Build it and confirm the compile succeeds:

  ```bash
  pnpm -C contracts/fixtures/plugin-live-dispatch install && pnpm -C contracts/fixtures/plugin-live-dispatch build && ls contracts/fixtures/plugin-live-dispatch/lib
  ```

  Expected: `index.js` exists. A type error here means a dsh agent API moved — record which symbol failed in this file and stop; do not "fix" it by loosening a type.

  describe.skipIf(!live)(
    "contract (live): one real deepseek-v4-flash turn",
    () => {

        ).toBeTruthy();
        dshHome = makeDshHome();
        execFileSync("pnpm", ["-C", fixtureDir, "install"], { stdio: "pipe" });
        execFileSync("pnpm", ["-C", fixtureDir, "build"], { stdio: "pipe" });
        const profileDir = join(dshHome, "profiles", "heliumlive");
        mkdirSync(profileDir, { recursive: true });
        writeFileSync(
          join(profileDir, "package.json"),
          `${JSON.stringify(
            {
              name: "dsh-profile-heliumlive",
              private: true,
              dsh: {
                profile: {
                  bundles: [
                    "@deepseek-ai/dsh-base",
                    "dsh-plugin-helium-live-dispatch",
                  ],
                },
              },
              dependencies: {
                "dsh-plugin-helium-live-dispatch": `file:${fixtureDir}`,
              },
            },
            undefined,
            2,
          )}\n`,
        );
        writeFileSync(join(profileDir, "cordis.patch.yml"), "[]\n");
        writeFileSync(
          join(profileDir, "pnpm-workspace.yaml"),
          "packages:\n  - .\n\nnodeLinker: hoisted\nautoInstallPeers: false\n",
        );
        execFileSync("pnpm", ["-C", profileDir, "install"], { stdio: "pipe" });
      });

      afterAll(() => {
        rmSync(dshHome, { recursive: true, force: true });
      });

      it("creates an agent, follows up, idles, and captures the final text", async () => {
        const outFile = join(dshHome, "live.jsonl");
        const child = spawn(dshBin, ["--profile", "heliumlive"], {
          env: { ...process.env, DSH_HOME: dshHome, HELIUM_LIVE_OUT: outFile },
          stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"],
        });
        const stderr: string[] = [];
        child.stderr.on("data", (chunk) => stderr.push(String(chunk)));
        const deadline = Date.now() + 180_000;
        try {
          while (Date.now() < deadline && !existsSync(outFile)) {
            await new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, 500));
          }
        } finally {
          child.kill("SIGTERM");
        }
        expect(
          existsSync(outFile),
          `no dispatch record; stderr:\n${stderr.join("")}`,
        ).toBe(true);
        const record = JSON.parse(readFileSync(outFile, "utf8").trim()) as {
          finalText: string;
          sessionId: string;
          latencyMs: number;
        };
        expect(record.sessionId).toMatch(/^session-/);
        expect(record.finalText.trim().length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
        expect(record.latencyMs).toBeGreaterThan(0);
      });
    },
  );
  ```

  Run it once, deliberately, on the laptop:

  ```bash
  HELIUM_LIVE=1 pnpm run test:contracts contracts/tests/agent-dispatch.live.contract.spec.ts
  ```

  Expected: `1 passed`, cost a few cents. Then confirm it is skipped by default:

  ```bash
  pnpm run test:contracts contracts/tests/agent-dispatch.live.contract.spec.ts
  ```

  Expected: `1 skipped`.

**Interfaces:**

**Library choice for spike B:** `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` — it is the MCP reference SDK and the one dsh itself depends on (`$DSH_EVAL/packages/mcp/mcp-client/package.json` pins `^1.12.0`), so helium's phase-2 `helium-mcp` server will use the same surface.

#### Spike A — does the dsh web UI coexist with helium's plugins in one process?

- [ ] **Step 1: Establish the correct invocation before testing anything.**
      The design originally assumed a `dsh --profile helium web` command. That form does not exist (spec §3 has been corrected from source; this spike supplies the empirical confirmation). `$DSH_EVAL/apps/cli/src/args.ts` defines `web` as a **top-level subcommand that is a hardcoded alias for `--profile web`**, and everything after the launcher's own flags is handed verbatim to the booted app. The browser surface is a _bundle_ (`@deepseek-ai/dsh-web-app`); `PROFILE_TEMPLATES.web` is exactly `['@deepseek-ai/dsh-base', '@deepseek-ai/dsh-web-app']` (`$DSH_EVAL/packages/boot/app-boot/src/profile.ts:114-117`). Confirm the negative first:

  ```bash
  HELIUM_TEST_HOME="$(mktemp -d /tmp/helium-spikeA.XXXXXX)"
  ./scripts/deploy-profile.sh --dsh-home "$HELIUM_TEST_HOME"
  DSH_HOME="$HELIUM_TEST_HOME" ./node_modules/.bin/dsh --profile helium web --dump-config; echo "exit=$?"
  ```

  Expected: a nonzero exit with `error: config dumps take no app arguments, got "web"` — proving `web` was treated as an app argument, not as a mode.

- [ ] **Step 2: Add the web bundle to the throwaway profile.**
      Edit `$HELIUM_TEST_HOME/profiles/helium/package.json` so `dsh.profile.bundles` reads
      `["@deepseek-ai/dsh-base", "@deepseek-ai/dsh-web-app", "dsh-plugin-helium"]`, then:

  ```bash
  DSH_HOME="$HELIUM_TEST_HOME" ./node_modules/.bin/dsh --profile helium --dump-config | grep -c 'dsh-plugin-helium'
  ```

  Expected: ≥1, and exit 0 — the web bundle and helium compose together.

- [ ] **Step 3: Boot it and probe both surfaces.**

  ```bash
  TICKS="$HELIUM_TEST_HOME/ticks.log"
  DSH_HOME="$HELIUM_TEST_HOME" HELIUM_CONTRACT_TICK_FILE="$TICKS" \
    ./node_modules/.bin/dsh --profile helium --port 3080 --no-open > "$HELIUM_TEST_HOME/dsh.log" 2>&1 &
  DSH_PID=$!
  sleep 15
  curl -sS -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' http://127.0.0.1:3080/
  grep -c 'helium plugin mounted' "$HELIUM_TEST_HOME/dsh.log"
  wc -l < "$TICKS"
  kill -TERM "$DSH_PID"; wait "$DSH_PID"; echo "exit=$?"
  ```

  Flags are grounded in `$DSH_EVAL/packages/bundle/web-app/README.md` (the web app owns `--host`, `--port`, `--trusted-host`, `--no-open`) and `$DSH_EVAL/apps/cli/reference/README.md` (default `http://127.0.0.1:3080`; loopback only, per spec §3 security).
  Expected on success: HTTP `200`, `1` for the mount log, a tick count ≥ 100, and `exit=0`. If the web bundle fails at activation with a "frontend dist" build hint (a documented limitation of `@deepseek-ai/dsh-web-app`), that is the verdict — record it as NO with the exact error, do not work around it.

- [x] **Step 4: Record the Spike A verdict here.**

  > **Spike A verdict (2026-08-23, dsh 0.1.1-rc.2):** YES — the dsh web UI served `127.0.0.1:3080` (HTTP `200`) while `dsh-plugin-helium` ran its `ctx.effect` timer in the same process (`122` ticks, mount log present: yes). Correct invocation is `dsh --profile helium --port 3080 --no-open` with `@deepseek-ai/dsh-web-app` listed in the profile's `dsh.profile.bundles`; `dsh --profile helium web` is not a valid form. One deviation from the plan skeleton's assumed npm state: `@deepseek-ai/dsh-web-app`'s npm `latest` dist-tag (`0.0.1-rc.1`) is stale and its dependency graph 404s on `@deepseek-ai/dsh-code-runtime-worker` (renamed downstream); the version that actually matches core `@deepseek-ai/dsh@0.1.1-rc.2` is published under the `next` dist-tag, `0.1.1-rc.2` — the throwaway profile pins that version explicitly. Evidence:
  >
  > ```
  > $ DSH_HOME="$HELIUM_TEST_HOME" ./node_modules/.bin/dsh --profile helium web --dump-config; echo "exit=$?"
  > error: web takes none of parent --profile, --patch, --dump-config, or --dump-default-config

  > $ DSH_HOME="$HELIUM_TEST_HOME" ./node_modules/.bin/dsh --profile helium --dump-config | grep -c 'dsh-plugin-helium'; echo "exit=$?"

  ```js
  // Spike B fixture: the smallest MCP stdio server that proves a `claude -p`
  // child can call a helium-owned tool. Phase 2 replaces it with helium-mcp.
  import { McpServer } from "@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/server/mcp.js";
  import { StdioServerTransport } from "@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/server/stdio.js";
  import { z } from "zod";

  const server = new McpServer({ name: "helium", version: "0.0.0" });

  server.tool(
    "helium_ping",
    "Liveness probe for the helium MCP bridge. Returns the string pong.",
    {},
    async () => ({ content: [{ type: "text", text: "pong" }] }),
  );

  await server.connect(new StdioServerTransport());
  ```

  Expected: `exit=0`, a JSON object whose `result` text mentions `pong`, and `grep -c` ≥ 1. `--strict-mcp-config` is included so the operator's other MCP servers cannot satisfy the call — that is what makes the round-trip attributable to our stub.

- [x] **Step 11: Record the Spike B verdict here and in the spec.**

  > **Spike B verdict (2026-08-23, claude 2.1.241):** YES — a `claude -p` child called `mcp__helium__helium_ping` over MCP stdio and returned `pong`. Flags used: `--mcp-config`, `--strict-mcp-config`, `--allowedTools`, `--output-format json`, `--max-turns 3` (present/absent: present — accepted by the binary with no unknown-option error, though still hidden from `--help` on 2.1.241, exactly as the plan skeleton predicted). Environment note: this laptop shell has `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set (the agent harness's own key), which shadows the `claude` CLI's subscription OAuth login and made the first attempt fail with a billing error ("Credit balance is too low", `apiKeySource: "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"`) — not a spike failure, an auth-precedence issue. Re-running with that variable unset for the one call (`env -u ANTHROPIC_API_KEY claude -p ...`) reached the logged-in subscription and succeeded. This validates spec §4's existing design choice to run helium's senior lane from an explicit `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` file rather than relying on ambient `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. Evidence (trimmed):

  >

  > {"is_error":false,"num_turns":3,"stop_reason":"end_turn","subtype":"success",
  >  "result":"`pong`\n\nThe helium MCP bridge is live.","type":"result"}
  > ```

## Phase 2 — Reactive core

Phase 2 turns the Phase 1 skeleton into a working reactive loop: sensors detect state changes
and calendar/cron windows, dispatch runs the two-tier triage → senior lanes with budgets and
single-flight, delivery writes JSONL/reports/email, and the ecosystem toolkit ships as one
implementation exposed twice (in-process dsh tools + MCP stdio for `claude -p`). All work
happens on `feat/phase2-core` and lands as one PR titled "Phase 2: reactive core".

**dsh API ground truth used by this phase.** Every dsh call below was verified in the dsh
`0.1.1-rc.2` source clone at
`/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-chenxi-projects-OpenAlice/1bd89add-f51b-41ad-8b97-b8b70c6323d3/scratchpad/dsh-eval`
(`$DSH_SRC` below) and/or in `docs/spikes/2026-08-23-dsh-spike/plugin-index.ts`. File:line
citations appear in the tasks that use them. If a cited symbol is missing from the repo's own
`node_modules/@deepseek-ai/*`, stop that task and record the gap — never invent a substitute.

### Task 2.1: Sensor — state-change poller

Pure field extraction + hashing, plus a `StateChangePoller` that owns baseline/dedup
persistence, and the cordis `ctx.effect` interval wiring copied verbatim from the spike.

**Files:**
- `plugins/helium/src/config.ts` — new: runtime `Config` contract + derived state paths.
- `plugins/helium/src/sensor.ts` — new: `extractFields`, `hashFields`, `StateChangePoller`.
- `plugins/helium/src/sensor.test.ts` — new.
- `plugins/helium/src/testing/http-fixture.ts` — new: throwaway `node:http` server for tests.
- `plugins/helium/src/index.ts` — modify: umbrella `apply()` wires the poller via `ctx.effect`.
- `plugins/helium/cordis.patch.yml` — modify: env-assembled config in the spike `!!js` format.
- `packages/core/src/index.ts` — ensure a barrel export so plugin code imports `@helium/core`.

**Interfaces:**

```ts
// plugins/helium/src/config.ts
export interface Config {
  jobsDir: string; stateRoot: string; contextFile: string; calendarsDir: string;
  argonBase: string; apexBase: string; livewireDb?: string; envFile: string;
  claudeTokenFile: string; proxy: string; mcpBin: string; emailTo: string;
}
export const ConfigSchema: z.ZodType<Config>;
export interface StatePaths {
  state: string; sensors: string; jsonl: string; reports: string; theses: string;
}
export function statePaths(cfg: Config): StatePaths;

// plugins/helium/src/sensor.ts
export function extractFields(body: unknown, fields: string[]): Record<string, unknown>;
export function hashFields(x: Record<string, unknown>): string;
export interface TriggerEvent {
  job: string; kind: Trigger['kind']; firedAt: string; dedupKey: string;
  payload: Record<string, unknown>;
}
export type PollState = 'baseline' | 'unchanged' | 'changed' | 'deduped' | 'unknown';
export interface PollStatus {
  job: string; url: string; state: PollState; hash?: string; error?: string;
}
export class StateChangePoller {
  constructor(opts: {
    job: string; trigger: TriggerStateChange; store: StateStore;
    onTrigger: (ev: TriggerEvent) => void | Promise<void>;
    fetchImpl?: typeof fetch; now?: () => Date;
  });
  tick(): Promise<PollStatus>;
}
```

```

If it does not already map `"."` to `./lib/index.js`, create `packages/core/src/index.ts`

- [ ] **Step 3** — Pin the plugin runtime config contract. Create `plugins/helium/src/config.ts`.

```ts
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { z } from 'zod';

export interface Config {
  jobsDir: string;
  stateRoot: string;
  contextFile: string;
  calendarsDir: string;
  argonBase: string;
  apexBase: string;
  livewireDb?: string;
  envFile: string;
  claudeTokenFile: string;
  proxy: string;
  mcpBin: string;
  emailTo: string;
}

export const ConfigSchema = z.object({
  jobsDir: z.string().min(1),
  stateRoot: z.string().min(1),
  contextFile: z.string().min(1),
  calendarsDir: z.string().min(1),
  argonBase: z.string().min(1),
  apexBase: z.string().min(1),
  livewireDb: z.string().min(1).optional(),
  envFile: z.string().min(1),
  claudeTokenFile: z.string().min(1),
  proxy: z.string().min(1),
  mcpBin: z.string().min(1),
  emailTo: z.string().min(1),
}) satisfies z.ZodType<Config>;

export interface StatePaths {
  state: string; sensors: string; jsonl: string; reports: string; theses: string;
}

/** HELIUM_STATE_ROOT points directly at the harness state dir (e.g. ~/.helium/state). */
export function statePaths(cfg: Config): StatePaths {
  const state = cfg.stateRoot;
  return {
    state,
    sensors: join(state, 'sensors'),
    jsonl: join(state, 'jsonl'),
    reports: join(state, 'reports'),
    theses: join(state, 'theses'),
  };
}
```

import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { extractFields, hashFields } from './sensor.js';

describe('extractFields', () => {
  it('resolves dot-paths, array indices and missing paths', () => {
    const body = {
      regime: { state: 'tightening', confidence: 0.72 },
      direction: 'up',
      legs: [{ tenor: '2y' }, { tenor: '10y' }],
    };
    expect(extractFields(body, ['regime.state', 'legs.1.tenor', 'missing.path'])).toEqual({
      'regime.state': 'tightening',
      'legs.1.tenor': '10y',
      'missing.path': null,
    });
  });

  it('returns null for every path when the body is not an object', () => {
    expect(extractFields('boom', ['a.b'])).toEqual({ 'a.b': null });
  });
});

describe('hashFields', () => {
  it('is a 12-char hex digest independent of key insertion order', () => {
    const a = hashFields({ 'regime.state': 'tightening', direction: 'up' });
    const b = hashFields({ direction: 'up', 'regime.state': 'tightening' });
    expect(a).toBe(b);
    expect(a).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{12}$/);
  });

  it('changes when any watched value changes', () => {
    expect(hashFields({ a: 1 })).not.toBe(hashFields({ a: 2 }));
  });
});

- [ ] **Step 6** — Implement the pure helpers in `plugins/helium/src/sensor.ts`.

```ts
import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
import { nowIso, type StateStore, type Trigger, type TriggerStateChange } from '@helium/core';

export interface TriggerEvent {
  job: string;
  kind: Trigger['kind'];
  firedAt: string;
  dedupKey: string;
  payload: Record<string, unknown>;
}

/**
 * Resolve each dot-path against `body`. A path that does not resolve yields `null`
 * (never `undefined`) so JSON.stringify keeps the key and the hash stays stable.
 */
export function extractFields(body: unknown, fields: string[]): Record<string, unknown> {
  const out: Record<string, unknown> = {};
  for (const path of fields) {
    let cursor: unknown = body;
    for (const segment of path.split('.')) {
      if (cursor === null || typeof cursor !== 'object') { cursor = undefined; break; }
      cursor = (cursor as Record<string, unknown>)[segment];
    }
    out[path] = cursor === undefined ? null : cursor;
  }
  return out;
}

/** sha256 over the key-sorted JSON projection, truncated to 12 hex chars. */
export function hashFields(x: Record<string, unknown>): string {
  const sorted = Object.keys(x).sort().map((k) => [k, x[k]] as const);
  return createHash('sha256').update(JSON.stringify(sorted)).digest('hex').slice(0, 12);
}
```
