- [ ] **Step 4** — Implement `packages/core/src/theses.ts`.

```ts
import { existsSync, mkdirSync, readFileSync, readlinkSync, renameSync, symlinkSync, unlinkSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { createTwoFilesPatch } from 'diff';
import { nowIso } from './time.js';

const MAX_BYTES = 64 * 1024;

export class ThesisStore {
  constructor(private readonly stateRoot: string) {}

  /** <stateDir>/theses/<job>/ — versions plus a `current` symlink (spec §7). */
  dir(job: string): string { return join(this.stateRoot, 'theses', job); }

  read(job: string): string | null {
    const current = join(this.dir(job), 'current');
    if (!existsSync(current)) return null;
    return readFileSync(current, 'utf8');
  }

  write(job: string, content: string): { path: string; diff: string } {
    const bytes = Buffer.byteLength(content, 'utf8');
    if (bytes > MAX_BYTES) {
      throw new Error(`thesis rewrite is ${bytes} bytes; the cap is 65536 (64 KiB)`);
    }
    const dir = this.dir(job);
    mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
    const previous = this.read(job) ?? '';
    const stamp = nowIso().replace(/[:.]/g, '-');
    const name = `${stamp}.md`;
    const path = join(dir, name);
    writeFileSync(path, content);

    // Atomic re-point: symlink to a temp name, then rename over `current`.
    const tmp = join(dir, `.current-${stamp}`);
    if (existsSync(tmp)) unlinkSync(tmp);
    symlinkSync(name, tmp);           // relative target keeps the tree relocatable
    renameSync(tmp, join(dir, 'current'));

    return {
      path,
      diff: createTwoFilesPatch('thesis/previous', 'thesis/current', previous, content,
        'previous', stamp, { context: 3 }),
    };
  }
}
```

```ts
import { mkdtempSync } from 'node:fs';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { buildTools, isSelectOnly } from './index.js';

describe('isSelectOnly', () => {
  it('accepts SELECT and WITH, rejects everything else and multi-statement input', () => {
    expect(isSelectOnly('SELECT 1')).toBe(true);
    expect(isSelectOnly('-- note\nWITH x AS (SELECT 1) SELECT * FROM x')).toBe(true);
    expect(isSelectOnly('SELECT 1;')).toBe(true);
    expect(isSelectOnly('DELETE FROM bars')).toBe(false);
    expect(isSelectOnly('SELECT 1; DROP TABLE bars')).toBe(false);
    expect(isSelectOnly('/* SELECT */ ATTACH \'x\'')).toBe(false);
  });
});

describe('ecosystem tools', () => {
  let fixture: Fixture;
  let seen: { url: string; method: string }[];

  beforeEach(async () => {
    seen = [];
    fixture = await startFixture((req, res) => {
      seen.push({ url: req.url ?? '', method: req.method ?? '' });
      json(res, { ok: true, path: req.url });
    });
  });
  afterEach(async () => { await fixture.close(); });

  const tools = () => buildTools({
    argonBase: fixture.url, apexBase: fixture.url,
    stateRoot: mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'helium-tools-')),
  });
  const byName = (n: string) => tools().find((t) => t.name === n)!;

  it('exposes exactly the expected tool set with the right mutation flags', () => {
    expect(tools().map((t) => t.name).sort()).toEqual([
      'apex_api', 'apex_compute', 'argon_ai_analysis', 'argon_api', 'argon_rescan',
      'livewire_sql', 'thesis_read', 'thesis_write',
    ]);
    expect(byName('argon_api').mutating).toBe(false);
    expect(byName('argon_rescan').mutating).toBe(true);
    expect(byName('argon_ai_analysis').mutating).toBe(true);
    expect(byName('apex_compute').mutating).toBe(false);
    expect(byName('thesis_write').mutating).toBe(false);
  });

  it('argon_api GETs an allow-listed path and refuses anything else', async () => {
    const out = JSON.parse(await byName('argon_api').run({ path: '/api/rates/snapshot' }));
    expect(out.status).toBe(200);
    expect(seen[0]).toEqual({ url: '/api/rates/snapshot', method: 'GET' });
    await expect(byName('argon_api').run({ path: '/api/admin/wipe' }))
      .rejects.toThrow(/not an allow-listed/);
    await expect(byName('argon_api').run({ path: 'https://evil.example.com/x' }))
      .rejects.toThrow(/must start with/);
  });

  it('argon_api appends query parameters', async () => {
    await byName('argon_api').run({ path: '/api/macro/rates', query: { window: '30d' } });
    expect(seen[0]?.url).toBe('/api/macro/rates?window=30d');
  });

  it('apex_api allows /health and /v1/* only', async () => {
    await byName('apex_api').run({ path: '/v1/bars', query: { symbol: 'SPY' } });
    expect(seen[0]?.url).toBe('/v1/bars?symbol=SPY');
    await expect(byName('apex_api').run({ path: '/api/v1/bars' })).rejects.toThrow(/must start with/);
  });

  it('argon_rescan and apex_compute POST only to their own allow-lists', async () => {
    for (const name of ['argon_rescan', 'argon_ai_analysis', 'apex_compute']) {
      await expect(byName(name).run({ path: '/api/anything' })).rejects.toThrow(/not an allow-listed/);
    }
  });

  it('thesis_write versions through ThesisStore and returns the diff', async () => {
    const t = tools();
    const write = t.find((x) => x.name === 'thesis_write')!;
    const read = t.find((x) => x.name === 'thesis_read')!;
    const first = JSON.parse(await write.run({ job: 'macro-watch', content: 'v1\n' }));
    expect(first.diff).toContain('+v1');
    expect(JSON.parse(await read.run({ job: 'macro-watch' })).content).toBe('v1\n');
  });

  it('livewire_sql refuses a non-SELECT before touching DuckDB', async () => {
    const t = buildTools({
      argonBase: fixture.url, apexBase: fixture.url, livewireDb: '/nonexistent.duckdb',
      stateRoot: mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'helium-tools-')),
    }).find((x) => x.name === 'livewire_sql')!;
    await expect(t.run({ sql: 'DROP TABLE bars' })).rejects.toThrow(/SELECT/);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 8** — Implement the HTTP tools. `packages/core/src/tools/types.ts` holds the
skeleton's `ToolRunContext`/`EcosystemTool` verbatim. `packages/core/src/tools/argon.ts`:

```ts
import { z } from 'zod';
import type { EcosystemTool, ToolRunContext } from './types.js';

/** Verified argon routes (2026-08-23). Read-only surface. */
export const ARGON_READ_PREFIXES = [
  '/api/macro/', '/api/rates/snapshot', '/api/gold/', '/api/regime', '/api/health', '/api/stock/',
] as const;

/**
 * Mutating argon routes, fail-closed. Populate from argon's own OpenAPI in Step 9 —
 * an empty list means every call is refused, which is the safe default.
 */
export const ARGON_RESCAN_PATHS: readonly string[] = [];
export const ARGON_AI_ANALYSIS_PATHS: readonly string[] = [];

const PathParams = z.object({
  path: z.string(),
  query: z.record(z.string(), z.string()).optional(),
});

export function buildUrl(base: string, path: string, query?: Record<string, string>): string {
  if (!path.startsWith('/')) throw new Error(`path must start with "/", got: ${path}`);
  const url = new URL(base.replace(/\/$/, '') + path);
  for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(query ?? {})) url.searchParams.set(k, v);
  return url.toString();
}

export async function call(
  url: string, method: 'GET' | 'POST', ctx?: ToolRunContext,
): Promise<string> {
  const impl = ctx?.fetchImpl ?? fetch;
  const res = await impl(url, { method, signal: AbortSignal.timeout(30_000) });
  const text = await res.text();
  let body: unknown;
  try { body = JSON.parse(text); } catch { body = text; }
  return JSON.stringify({ status: res.status, url, body });
}

function readTool(
  name: string, description: string, base: string, prefixes: readonly string[],
): EcosystemTool {
  return {
    name, description, paramsSchema: PathParams, mutating: false,
    async run(args, ctx) {
      const { path, query } = PathParams.parse(args);
      if (!prefixes.some((p) => path.startsWith(p))) {
        throw new Error(`${name}: "${path}" is not an allow-listed read path (${prefixes.join(', ')})`);
      }
      return await call(buildUrl(base, path, query), 'GET', ctx);
    },
  };
}

function postTool(
  name: string, description: string, base: string, allowed: readonly string[], mutating: boolean,
): EcosystemTool {
  return {
    name, description, paramsSchema: PathParams, mutating,
    async run(args, ctx) {
      const { path, query } = PathParams.parse(args);
      if (!allowed.includes(path)) {
        throw new Error(`${name}: "${path}" is not an allow-listed path for this tool`);
      }
      return await call(buildUrl(base, path, query), 'POST', ctx);
    },
  };
}

export function argonTools(argonBase: string): EcosystemTool[] {
  return [
    readTool('argon_api',
      'GET a read-only argon route. Allowed prefixes: /api/macro/, /api/rates/snapshot, ' +
      '/api/gold/, /api/regime, /api/health, /api/stock/. Returns {status, url, body}.',
      argonBase, ARGON_READ_PREFIXES),
    postTool('argon_rescan',
      'POST an argon rescan job. Mutating: requires the job\'s allowMutations flag.',
      argonBase, ARGON_RESCAN_PATHS, true),
    postTool('argon_ai_analysis',
      'POST an argon ai-analysis job. Mutating: requires the job\'s allowMutations flag.',
      argonBase, ARGON_AI_ANALYSIS_PATHS, true),
  ];
}
```

`packages/core/src/tools/apex.ts` mirrors it with `APEX_READ_PREFIXES = ['/health', '/v1/']`,
`APEX_COMPUTE_PATHS: readonly string[] = []`, and `apex_compute` built by `postTool(..., false)`
whose description states: *"POST-to-enqueue compute (screener/backtest). Mutates no domain
state but is expensive — do not call more than once per analysis."*

- [ ] **Step 10** — Implement `packages/core/src/tools/livewire.ts` and
`packages/core/src/tools/thesis.ts`.

```ts
// livewire.ts
import { DuckDBInstance } from '@duckdb/node-api';
import { z } from 'zod';
import type { EcosystemTool } from './types.js';

const Params = z.object({ sql: z.string().min(1), maxRows: z.number().int().min(1).max(5_000).optional() });

/** Strip comments, then require a single SELECT/WITH statement. DuckDB READ_ONLY is layer two. */
export function isSelectOnly(sql: string): boolean {
  const bare = sql.replace(/\/\*[\s\S]*?\*\//g, ' ').replace(/--[^\n]*/g, ' ').trim();
  const withoutTrailing = bare.replace(/;\s*$/, '');
  if (withoutTrailing.includes(';')) return false;
  return /^(select|with)\b/i.test(withoutTrailing);
}

export function livewireTools(livewireDb: string | undefined): EcosystemTool[] {
  if (!livewireDb) return [];
  return [{
    name: 'livewire_sql',
    description:
      'Run one read-only SELECT (or WITH ... SELECT) against livewire\'s DuckDB lake. ' +
      'The connection is opened READ_ONLY; writes are refused by the engine.',
    paramsSchema: Params,
    mutating: false,
    async run(args) {
      const { sql, maxRows } = Params.parse(args);
      if (!isSelectOnly(sql)) {
        throw new Error('livewire_sql accepts a single SELECT or WITH statement only');
      }
      const instance = await DuckDBInstance.create(livewireDb, { access_mode: 'READ_ONLY' });
      const connection = await instance.connect();
      try {
        const reader = await connection.runAndReadAll(sql);
        const rows = reader.getRowObjectsJson().slice(0, maxRows ?? 200);
        return JSON.stringify({ rowCount: rows.length, rows });
      } finally {
        connection.closeSync();
        instance.closeSync();
      }
    },
  }];
}
```

```ts
// thesis.ts
import { z } from 'zod';
import { ThesisStore } from '../theses.js';
import type { EcosystemTool } from './types.js';

const ReadParams = z.object({ job: z.string().min(1) });
const WriteParams = z.object({ job: z.string().min(1), content: z.string() });

/** helium-owned state: versioned, capped and diffed, so mutating stays false by design. */
export function thesisTools(stateRoot: string): EcosystemTool[] {
  const store = new ThesisStore(stateRoot);
  return [
    {
      name: 'thesis_read',
      description: 'Read the current thesis for a job. Returns {content} or {content: null}.',
      paramsSchema: ReadParams, mutating: false,
      async run(args) {
        const { job } = ReadParams.parse(args);
        return JSON.stringify({ content: store.read(job) });
      },
    },
    {
      name: 'thesis_write',
      description:
        'Rewrite the thesis for a job. The write is versioned and capped at 64 KiB; the ' +
        'returned unified diff is included in the delivery email. Never edit the file directly.',
      paramsSchema: WriteParams, mutating: false,
      async run(args) {
        const { job, content } = WriteParams.parse(args);
        return JSON.stringify(store.write(job, content));
      },
    },
  ];
}
```

`packages/core/src/tools/index.ts` assembles them:

```ts
export function buildTools(cfg: {
  argonBase: string; apexBase: string; livewireDb?: string; stateRoot: string;
}): EcosystemTool[] {
  return [
    ...argonTools(cfg.argonBase),
    ...apexTools(cfg.apexBase),
    ...livewireTools(cfg.livewireDb),
    ...thesisTools(cfg.stateRoot),
  ];
}
```

- [ ] **Step 12** — Point the MCP server at `buildTools()`. Open
`packages/core/src/mcp/server.ts` (Phase 1 Task 1.7 created it against
`@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`); keep whatever `registerTool` call shape it already uses and
replace its tool list with the filtered `buildTools()` output. If the file does not exist,
create it exactly as below and add the `helium-mcp` bin to `packages/core/package.json`.

```ts
#!/usr/bin/env node
import { McpServer } from '@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/server/mcp.js';
import { StdioServerTransport } from '@modelcontextprotocol/sdk/server/stdio.js';
import { z } from 'zod';
import { buildTools } from '../tools/index.js';

function selected(): ReturnType<typeof buildTools> {
  const tools = buildTools({
    argonBase: process.env.HELIUM_ARGON_BASE ?? 'http://127.0.0.1:8400',
    apexBase: process.env.HELIUM_APEX_BASE ?? 'http://127.0.0.1:8322',
    livewireDb: process.env.HELIUM_LIVEWIRE_DB,
    stateRoot: process.env.HELIUM_STATE_ROOT ?? process.cwd(),
  });
  const allowMutations = process.env.HELIUM_ALLOW_MUTATIONS === '1';
  const names = (process.env.HELIUM_TOOLS ?? '').split(',').map((s) => s.trim()).filter(Boolean);
  return tools
    .filter((t) => allowMutations || !t.mutating)
    .filter((t) => names.length === 0 || names.includes(t.name));
}

const server = new McpServer({ name: 'helium', version: '0.2.0' });
for (const tool of selected()) {
  const shape = tool.paramsSchema instanceof z.ZodObject
    ? (tool.paramsSchema.shape as z.ZodRawShape)
    : ({} as z.ZodRawShape);
  server.registerTool(tool.name, { description: tool.description, inputSchema: shape },
    async (args: Record<string, unknown>) => ({
      content: [{ type: 'text' as const, text: await tool.run(args) }],
    }));
}
await server.connect(new StdioServerTransport());
```

```bash
{"jsonrpc":"2.0","method":"notifications/initialized"}
```

- [ ] **Step 13** — Register the same tools in-process for dsh. The API is confirmed in
`$DSH_SRC`: the service is `ctx.tools` (`packages/core/tools/src/index.ts:137-140`), the method
is `ToolRuntime.register(definition: ToolDefinition): () => void` (`:1037`), `output` is
**mandatory** (`register` throws a `TypeError` without it, `:1040-1044`), and the authoring
helper is `defineTool(options)` from `@deepseek-ai/dsh-tools`
(`packages/core/tools/src/schema.ts:545`, options interface at `:483`). Tool parameters are
**not** zod: they are a `ParameterSchemaSpec` DSL (`schema.ts:97-103`) — a flat map of
property → `{ type, description?, required?: true, ... }` where object nodes must set
`additionalProperties`. The canonical worked example is
`$DSH_SRC/packages/todo/tool-todo/src/index.ts:149-225`.

Because our params are flat and few, hand-write the dsh spec per tool rather than building a
zod→DSL compiler. Create `plugins/helium/src/toolkit.ts`:

```ts
import { defineTool } from '@deepseek-ai/dsh-tools';
import type { Context } from '@deepseek-ai/cordis';
import type { EcosystemTool } from '@helium/core';

/** dsh parameter specs, one per tool name. Kept in lockstep with buildTools() by a test. */
const DSH_PARAMS = {
  argon_api: {
    path: { type: 'string', required: true, description: 'Read-only argon path, e.g. /api/rates/snapshot' },
    query: { type: 'object', additionalProperties: true, description: 'Optional query parameters' },
  },
  argon_rescan: {
    path: { type: 'string', required: true, description: 'Allow-listed argon rescan path' },
  },
  argon_ai_analysis: {
    path: { type: 'string', required: true, description: 'Allow-listed argon ai-analysis path' },
  },
  apex_api: {
    path: { type: 'string', required: true, description: 'apex path: /health or /v1/...' },
    query: { type: 'object', additionalProperties: true, description: 'Optional query parameters' },
  },
  apex_compute: {
    path: { type: 'string', required: true, description: 'Allow-listed apex compute path' },
  },
  livewire_sql: {
    sql: { type: 'string', required: true, description: 'A single read-only SELECT or WITH statement' },
    maxRows: { type: 'integer', description: 'Row cap, default 200' },
  },
  thesis_read: { job: { type: 'string', required: true, description: 'Job name' } },
  thesis_write: {
    job: { type: 'string', required: true, description: 'Job name' },
    content: { type: 'string', required: true, description: 'The full rewritten thesis, max 64 KiB' },
  },
} as const;

export type ToolkitCtx = Pick<Context, 'tools'>;

export function registerEcosystemTools(ctx: ToolkitCtx, tools: EcosystemTool[]): void {
  for (const tool of tools) {
    const parameters = DSH_PARAMS[tool.name as keyof typeof DSH_PARAMS];
    if (!parameters) throw new Error(`toolkit: no dsh parameter spec for tool "${tool.name}"`);
    ctx.tools.register(defineTool({
      name: tool.name,
      description: tool.description,
      parameters,
      output: {
        schema: { type: 'string' },
        render: (_args, value) => [{ type: 'text', text: String(value) }],
      },
      execute: async (args) => await tool.run(args as Record<string, unknown>),
    }));
  }
}
```

Test it with a fake `ctx.tools`, `plugins/helium/src/toolkit.test.ts`:

```ts
import { mkdtempSync } from 'node:fs';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { buildTools } from '@helium/core';
import { registerEcosystemTools } from './toolkit.js';

const tools = () => buildTools({
  argonBase: 'http://127.0.0.1:8400', apexBase: 'http://127.0.0.1:8322',
  livewireDb: '/tmp/livewire.duckdb', stateRoot: mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'helium-tk-')),
});

describe('registerEcosystemTools', () => {
  it('registers every tool buildTools produces', () => {
    const registered: { name: string }[] = [];
    registerEcosystemTools(
      { tools: { register: (d: { name: string }) => { registered.push(d); return () => {}; } } } as never,
      tools(),
    );
    expect(registered.map((d) => d.name).sort()).toEqual(tools().map((t) => t.name).sort());
  });

  it('fails loudly when a tool has no dsh parameter spec', () => {
    expect(() => registerEcosystemTools(
      { tools: { register: () => () => {} } } as never,
      [{ name: 'unmapped', description: '', paramsSchema: {} as never, mutating: false, run: async () => '' }],
    )).toThrow(/no dsh parameter spec/);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 14** — Write `context/ecosystem.md`, the layer-1 context injected into every agent.

```markdown
# The ecosystem helium watches

You are an analyst inside a private trading research stack running on one Mac mini. Four
systems produce the data you reason over. You never place trades, and you never write to any
of them except through the tools named below.

## argon — macro and rates state (REST, 127.0.0.1:8400)

argon reconstructs the macro regime: policy stance, inflation trend, the rate path, the dollar,
and gold. It is the primary source for "what is the macro state right now".

- `GET /api/regime` — the current regime classification (state, direction, confidence).
- `GET /api/rates/snapshot` — the rate-path snapshot: front end vs long end, implied path.
- `GET /api/macro/policy` — policy stance evidence (FOMC language, dots, guidance).
- `GET /api/macro/inflation` — inflation trend and its components.
- `GET /api/macro/rates` — the rates detail behind the snapshot.
- `GET /api/macro/usd` — dollar positioning and trend.
- `GET /api/macro/gold` — gold as a macro instrument.
- `GET /api/gold/*` — the deeper gold surfaces.
- `GET /api/stock/{ticker}/trade-insights` — per-ticker context.
- `GET /api/health` — liveness and freshness.

Semantics: argon fields are *narrative state with a confidence*, not price data. `confidence`
is argon's own; do not re-derive it as a probability. A missing or stale field means unknown —
never treat a failed fetch or a stale timestamp as evidence of a regime change. Reach argon
with the `argon_api` tool. `argon_rescan` and `argon_ai_analysis` mutate and exist only for
jobs that explicitly enable mutations.

## apex — signals and chart data (REST, 127.0.0.1:8322, root-mounted, no /api prefix)

apex is the signal computation engine: bars, indicators, rule-based signals, confluence.

- `GET /health` — liveness.
- `GET /v1/bars`, `/v1/indicators`, `/v1/signals`, `/v1/confluence` — the read surfaces.
- Screener and backtest are POST-to-enqueue compute jobs (`apex_compute`). They mutate no
  domain state but cost real compute: at most one per analysis, and only when the question
  genuinely needs it.

Semantics: apex is deterministic and mechanical. It tells you which rules fired, not what they
mean. Use it to check whether a macro narrative has a price-level counterpart.

## livewire — the historical Parquet lake (DuckDB, local disk)

livewire is the durable market-data warehouse. helium reads it through a read-only DuckDB
connection over the Parquet lake with `livewire_sql`: one `SELECT` (or `WITH ... SELECT`) per
call, row-capped. Writes are refused by the engine, not merely by policy.

Semantics: the lake is point-in-time history. It is the right place for "has this happened
before", "what did the distribution look like", "how large is this move against its own
history". It is the wrong place for anything about the current session.

## signal-lab — research entry points (not wired in v1)

signal-lab owns the research scripts behind apex's rules. helium has no tool for it yet. If an
analysis would need it, say so in your answer rather than inventing a result.

## Your standing thesis

Jobs with `memory: thesis-file` carry a thesis: the current rate-path view, its confidence, and
what would falsify it. It is injected above your job prompt when one exists. Update it only
through the `thesis_write` tool, which versions the file, caps it at 64 KiB, and puts the diff
in the operator's email. Never edit a thesis file directly, and never rewrite it wholesale when
one clause changed.

## Rules

- Read-only by default. Every mutating tool is gated per job and audited.
- No trading writes exist anywhere in helium. Position and order questions are out of scope.
- A timeout or an error status means unknown — never "down", never "unchanged".
- Name the tool and route behind every number you state. If you did not fetch it, do not state it.
- Be terse. The operator reads these in an inbox, not in a terminal.
```

`ReasoningEffortId` finding from Task 2.3 Step 1, the `AgentHandle.dispose()` durability
finding from Task 2.3 Step 10, whether the argon/apex mutating allow-lists could be filled in
Task 2.7 Step 9, and the fact that a timed-out lane is abandoned rather than cancelled. None of
these block the phase; all are Phase 3 inputs.

---

## Phase 2 exit criteria

- `npx tsc --noEmit` and `pnpm test` are green, and both packages build to `lib/`.
- A cold `StateChangePoller` never fires; a real change fires exactly once inside its TTL.
- A cron schedule with an explicit IANA tz resolves correctly across a DST boundary.
- Triage produces a parsed verdict or a `run_failed` carrying `parse_error` — never a prose guess.
- A senior child is spawned with `--output-format json --max-turns N --allowedTools ...`; its
  proxy and token exist only in the child env; 403/401/timeout are classified distinctly.
- Every dispatch writes `run_started` then exactly one terminal phase, and every delivery
  writes its JSONL row before any report or email.
- `buildTools()` and the MCP server expose the same tool set, read-only by default.
## Phase 3 — Macro tenant & production

Wire the Phase 1/2 pieces into one running harness, give it its first real tenant
(macro-watch against argon), and put it on the Mac mini under launchd with an
external dead-man, versioned releases, an upgrade canary, and a signed-off
acceptance pass. Phase 3 is the first phase that writes to the mini; every mini
step below is shown as the full `ssh macmini` command that will run it.

Phase 3 implements spec §10 (canary), §11 (macro v1 scope), §12 (deployment,
secrets, release process) and acceptance criteria §13.2–§13.7, and starts the
§13.1 five-trading-day observation clock.

**Branch:** all seven tasks commit to `feat/phase3-production`. Task 3.7 opens the
PR, waits for green CI, and merges with a **merge commit** (not squash) so the
drill tags cut during 3.5–3.7 stay reachable from `master`.

**Pinned path contract for this phase** (used by every task; do not re-derive):

| name                | value on the mini                                                     |
| ------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `HELIUM_STATE_ROOT` | `/Users/moremeds/.helium/state`                                       |
| `DSH_HOME`          | `/Users/moremeds/.helium/dsh-home`                                    |

| live release        | `/Users/moremeds/projects/helium-releases/current` (symlink)          |
| previous release    | `/Users/moremeds/projects/helium-releases/previous` (symlink)         |
| mini source clone   | `/Users/moremeds/projects/helium`                                     |
| logs                | `/Users/moremeds/.helium/logs/`                                       |
| secrets             | `/Users/moremeds/.config/helium/{helium.env,claude-token.env}` (0600) |

State layout under `HELIUM_STATE_ROOT`: `jsonl/` (`runs-`, `heartbeat-`,

- [ ] **Step 2** — Write `plugins/helium/src/calendar.ts` (schema + window math):

```ts
import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { join } from "node:path";
import { parse } from "yaml";
import { z } from "zod";

export const CalendarEventSchema = z.object({
  id: z.string().min(1),
  kind: z.enum(["FOMC", "CPI", "NFP"]),
  at: z.string().datetime({ offset: true }),
  label: z.string().min(1),
  source: z.string().url(),
});
export const CalendarSchema = z.object({
  tz: z.string().min(1),
  events: z.array(CalendarEventSchema),
});
export type CalendarEvent = z.infer<typeof CalendarEventSchema>;

export function loadCalendar(dir: string, name: string): CalendarEvent[] {
  const text = readFileSync(join(dir, `${name}.yaml`), "utf8");
  return CalendarSchema.parse(parse(text)).events;
}

/** The event whose [at-before, at+after] window contains `now`, if any. */
export function activeWindow(
  events: readonly CalendarEvent[],
  beforeMs: number,
  afterMs: number,
  now: number,
): CalendarEvent | undefined {
  return events.find((e) => {
    const at = Date.parse(e.at);
    return now >= at - beforeMs && now <= at + afterMs;
  });
}
```

```ts
import {
  mkdirSync,
  readdirSync,
  readFileSync,
  rmSync,
  writeFileSync,
} from "node:fs";
import { join } from "node:path";
import {
  JsonlWriter,
  RunLedger,
  StateStore,
  ThesisStore,
  loadJobs,
  meetsThreshold,
  nowIso,
  parseVerdict,
  type JobSpec,
  type TriageVerdict,
} from "@helium/core";
import { extractFields, hashFields, type TriggerEvent } from "./sensor.js";
import type { DispatchResult } from "./dispatch.js";
import { activeWindow, loadCalendar, type CalendarEvent } from "./calendar.js";

export interface RuntimeConfig {
  jobsDir: string;
  stateRoot: string;
  contextFile: string;
  calendarsDir: string;
  argonBase: string;
  apexBase: string;
  livewireDb?: string;
  envFile: string;
  claudeTokenFile: string;
  proxy: string;
  mcpBin: string;
  emailTo: string;
}
export interface EngineReply {
  ok: boolean;
  text?: string;
  classification?: "proxy" | "auth" | "timeout" | "error";
  error?: string;
}
export interface EnginePorts {
  triage(job: JobSpec, prompt: string): Promise<EngineReply>;
  senior(job: JobSpec, prompt: string): Promise<EngineReply>;
  script?(job: JobSpec, ev: TriggerEvent): Promise<EngineReply>;
}
export interface DeliveryPorts {
  deliver(job: JobSpec, ev: TriggerEvent, r: DispatchResult): Promise<void>;
  heartbeat(row: Record<string, unknown>): void;
}
export interface RuntimeDeps {
  config: RuntimeConfig;
  engines: EnginePorts;
  delivery: DeliveryPorts;
  fetchImpl?: typeof fetch;
  now?: () => number;
  log?: (msg: string, extra?: Record<string, unknown>) => void;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4** — Write `runtime.ts` part 2 (the class: startup, sensors, heartbeat):

```ts
export class HeliumRuntime {
  private readonly jobs: JobSpec[];
  private readonly writer: JsonlWriter;
  private readonly ledger: RunLedger;
  private readonly state: StateStore;
  private readonly theses: ThesisStore;
  private readonly timers = new Set<NodeJS.Timeout>();
  private readonly inflight = new Set<string>();
  private readonly queued = new Map<string, TriggerEvent>();
  private readonly boost = new Map<string, CalendarEvent>();
  private seniorInFlight = 0;

  constructor(private readonly deps: RuntimeDeps) {
    const c = deps.config;
    for (const d of ["jsonl", "sensors", "theses", "reports", "inbox"]) {
      mkdirSync(join(c.stateRoot, d), { recursive: true });
    }
    this.writer = new JsonlWriter(join(c.stateRoot, "jsonl"));
    this.ledger = new RunLedger(this.writer);
    this.state = new StateStore(c.stateRoot);
    this.theses = new ThesisStore(c.stateRoot);
    this.jobs = loadJobs(c.jobsDir).filter((j) => j.enabled);
  }

  private nowMs(): number {
    return this.deps.now?.() ?? Date.now();
  }
  private fetch(): typeof fetch {
    return this.deps.fetchImpl ?? globalThis.fetch;
  }
  private log(m: string, x?: Record<string, unknown>): void {
    (this.deps.log ?? ((s, e) => console.log(`[helium] ${s}`, e ?? "")))(m, x);
  }

  start(): void {
    const interrupted = this.ledger.reconcileStartup();
    this.log("startup reconciled", {
      interrupted,
      jobs: this.jobs.map((j) => j.name),
    });
    this.writer.append("runs", {
      phase: "harness_started",
      interrupted,
      jobs: this.jobs.length,
    });
    for (const job of this.jobs) this.startJob(job);
  }

  stop(): void {
    for (const t of this.timers) clearInterval(t);
    this.timers.clear();
  }

  private every(ms: number, fn: () => void): void {
    const t = setInterval(() => {
      void Promise.resolve(fn()).catch((e: unknown) =>
        this.log("timer error", { error: String(e) }),
      );
    }, ms);
    t.unref?.();
    this.timers.add(t);
  }

  private startJob(job: JobSpec): void {
    for (const trig of job.triggers) {
      if (trig.kind === "state-change") {
        this.every(trig.intervalMs, () => this.pollStateChange(job, trig));
        void this.pollStateChange(job, trig); // establish baseline immediately
      } else if (trig.kind === "calendar-window") {
        this.every(trig.intervalDuringMs, () => this.tickCalendar(job, trig));
      } else {
        this.every(30_000, () => this.tickCron(job, trig));
      }
    }
  }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5** — Write `runtime.ts` part 3 (state-change poll: baseline, dedup, heartbeat):

```ts
  private async pollStateChange(job: JobSpec, trig: TriggerStateChange): Promise<void> {
    const key = `${job.name}::${trig.url}`;
    let status: "ok" | "unknown" = "ok";
    let fired = false;
    try {
      const res = await this.fetch()(trig.url, { signal: AbortSignal.timeout(10_000) });
      if (!res.ok) throw new Error(`HTTP ${res.status}`);
      const body: unknown = await res.json();
      const fields = extractFields(body, trig.fields);
      const hash = hashFields(fields);
      const st = this.state.loadSensor(job.name);
      const prev = st.baseline;
      st.baseline = { hash, fields };
      if (!prev) {
        this.state.saveSensor(job.name, st);           // cold start: never fires
      } else if (prev.hash !== hash) {
        const dedupKey = `${trig.url}#${hash}`;
        const until = st.dedup[dedupKey];
        const nowIsoStr = nowIso();
        if (until && Date.parse(until) > this.nowMs()) {
          this.writer.append("triggers", { job: job.name, url: trig.url,
            suppressed: "dedup", dedupKey });
        } else {
          st.dedup[dedupKey] = new Date(this.nowMs() + trig.dedupTtlMs).toISOString();
          fired = true;
          const ev: TriggerEvent = { job: job.name, kind: "state-change",
            firedAt: nowIsoStr, dedupKey,
            payload: { url: trig.url, previous: prev.fields, current: fields } };
          this.state.saveSensor(job.name, st);
          this.writer.append("triggers", { job: job.name, url: trig.url, dedupKey });
          void this.handleTrigger(job, ev);
        }
      } else {
        this.state.saveSensor(job.name, st);
      }
    } catch (e: unknown) {
      status = "unknown";                               // a timeout is never 'down'
      this.log("poll failed", { job: job.name, url: trig.url, error: String(e) });
    }
    this.deps.delivery.heartbeat({ job: job.name, url: trig.url, status, fired,
      boosted: this.boost.has(job.name), inflight: this.inflight.size });
  }
```

Note `st.dedup` is pruned of expired keys by `StateStore.saveSensor` (Phase 1);
if it is not, add the prune here — do not let the map grow unbounded.

- [ ] **Step 6** — Write `runtime.ts` part 4 (calendar + cron ticks + inbox drain):

```ts
  private tickCalendar(job: JobSpec, trig: TriggerCalendarWindow): void {
    const events = loadCalendar(this.deps.config.calendarsDir, trig.calendar);
    const open = activeWindow(events, trig.beforeMs, trig.afterMs, this.nowMs());
    const was = this.boost.get(job.name);
    if (open && was?.id !== open.id) {
      this.boost.set(job.name, open);
      void this.handleTrigger(job, { job: job.name, kind: "calendar-window",
        firedAt: nowIso(), dedupKey: `calendar:${open.id}`,
        payload: { event: open, phase: "window-open" } });
    }
    if (!open && was) this.boost.delete(job.name);
    if (open) {
      for (const t of job.triggers) {
        if (t.kind === "state-change") void this.pollStateChange(job, t);
      }
    }
  }

  private tickCron(job: JobSpec, trig: TriggerCron): void {
    const st = this.state.loadSensor(job.name);
    const slot = cronSlot(trig.schedule, trig.tz, this.nowMs()); // '' when not due
    if (slot === "" || st.dedup[`cron:${slot}`]) return;
    st.dedup[`cron:${slot}`] = new Date(this.nowMs() + 86_400_000).toISOString();
    this.state.saveSensor(job.name, st);
    void this.handleTrigger(job, { job: job.name, kind: "cron", firedAt: nowIso(),
      dedupKey: `cron:${slot}`,
      payload: { schedule: trig.schedule, tz: trig.tz, inbox: this.drainInbox() } });
  }

  /** Out-of-process notices (canary etc.) folded into the next cron synthesis. */
  private drainInbox(): Record<string, unknown>[] {
    const dir = join(this.deps.config.stateRoot, "inbox");
    const out: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];
    for (const f of readdirSync(dir).filter((n) => n.endsWith(".json")).sort()) {
      const p = join(dir, f);
      try { out.push(JSON.parse(readFileSync(p, "utf8")) as Record<string, unknown>); }
      catch { /* leave malformed drops in place for inspection */ continue; }
      rmSync(p);
    }
    return out;
  }
```

`cronSlot(schedule, tz, nowMs)` is a small helper in `runtime.ts`: it renders
`nowMs` in `tz` with `Intl.DateTimeFormat`, matches the 5-field cron expression
restricted to the fields v1 uses (minute, hour, day-of-month `*`, month `*`,
day-of-week list/range), and returns `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm` in `tz` when due, `''`
otherwise. Reject any schedule it cannot parse at job-load time.

- [ ] **Step 7** — Write `runtime.ts` part 5 (dispatch: single-flight, budgets, two tiers):

```ts
  private async handleTrigger(job: JobSpec, ev: TriggerEvent): Promise<void> {
    if (this.inflight.has(job.name)) { this.queued.set(job.name, ev); return; }
    this.inflight.add(job.name);
    try {
      const result = await this.dispatch(job, ev);
      await this.deps.delivery.deliver(job, ev, result);
    } catch (e: unknown) {
      this.log("dispatch error", { job: job.name, error: String(e) });
    } finally {
      this.inflight.delete(job.name);
      const next = this.queued.get(job.name);
      if (next) { this.queued.delete(job.name); void this.handleTrigger(job, next); }
    }
  }

  private budgetOk(job: JobSpec, tier: "triage" | "senior"): boolean {
    const st = this.state.loadSensor(job.name);
    const windowMs = tier === "triage" ? 3_600_000 : 86_400_000;
    const cap = tier === "triage" ? job.budget.maxTriagePerHour : job.budget.maxSeniorPerDay;
    const list = (tier === "triage" ? st.triageFires : st.seniorFires)
      .filter((t) => this.nowMs() - Date.parse(t) < windowMs);
    if (list.length >= cap) {
      this.writer.append("runs", { job: job.name, tier, phase: "budget-exhausted",
        used: list.length, cap });
      return false;
    }
    list.push(new Date(this.nowMs()).toISOString());
    if (tier === "triage") st.triageFires = list; else st.seniorFires = list;
    this.state.saveSensor(job.name, st);
    return true;
  }

  private async dispatch(job: JobSpec, ev: TriggerEvent): Promise<DispatchResult> {
    if (job.script && this.deps.engines.script) return this.runScript(job, ev);
    if (!this.budgetOk(job, "triage")) {
      return { runId: "", tier: "triage", outcome: "run_failed", error: "budget" };
    }
    const runId = this.ledger.start(job.name, "triage");
    const reply = await this.deps.engines.triage(job, this.prompt(job, ev, "triage"));
    if (!reply.ok) {
      this.ledger.finish(runId, job.name, "triage", "run_failed", { error: reply.error });
      return { runId, tier: "triage", outcome: "run_failed", error: reply.error };
    }
    const verdict = parseVerdict(reply.text ?? "");
    this.ledger.finish(runId, job.name, "triage", "run_completed", { verdict });
    if (!verdict || !meetsThreshold(verdict, job.escalateWhen)) {
      return { runId, tier: "triage", outcome: "run_completed",
        verdict: verdict ?? undefined };
    }
    return this.escalate(job, ev, verdict);
  }
```

- [ ] **Step 8** — Write `runtime.ts` part 6 (senior escalation, prompt layering, report file):

```ts
  private async escalate(job: JobSpec, ev: TriggerEvent, v: TriageVerdict):
    Promise<DispatchResult> {
    if (this.seniorInFlight >= 2 || !this.budgetOk(job, "senior")) {
      return { runId: "", tier: "senior", outcome: "run_failed", verdict: v,
        error: "senior capacity or budget" };
    }
    this.seniorInFlight += 1;
    const runId = this.ledger.start(job.name, "senior");
    try {
      const reply = await this.deps.engines.senior(job, this.prompt(job, ev, "senior", v));
      const outcome = reply.ok ? "run_completed"
        : reply.classification === "timeout" ? "timed_out" : "run_failed";
      this.ledger.finish(runId, job.name, "senior", outcome,
        { classification: reply.classification });
      if (!reply.ok) {
        return { runId, tier: "senior", outcome, verdict: v, error: reply.error };
      }
      const dir = join(this.deps.config.stateRoot, "reports", job.name);
      mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
      const path = join(dir, `${nowIso().replace(/[:.]/g, "-")}.md`);
      writeFileSync(path, reply.text ?? "", "utf8");
      return { runId, tier: "senior", outcome: "run_completed", verdict: v,
        analysis: reply.text };
    } finally {
      this.seniorInFlight -= 1;
    }
  }

  /** Three-layer injection: ecosystem context -> job prompt -> trigger payload. */
  private prompt(job: JobSpec, ev: TriggerEvent, tier: "triage" | "senior",
    v?: TriageVerdict): string {
    const ctx = readFileSync(this.deps.config.contextFile, "utf8");
    const thesis = job.memory === "thesis-file" ? this.theses.read(job.name) : null;
    return [
      `# LAYER 1 — ECOSYSTEM CONTEXT\n${ctx}`,
      `# LAYER 2 — JOB (${job.name}, tier=${tier})\n${job.prompt}`,
      thesis ? `# LAYER 2b — STANDING THESIS\n${thesis}` : "",
      `# LAYER 3 — TRIGGER EVENT\n${JSON.stringify(ev, null, 2)}`,
      v ? `# TRIAGE VERDICT\n${JSON.stringify(v)}` : "",
    ].filter(Boolean).join("\n\n");
  }
```

`runScript(job, ev)` (used by the canary in Task 3.6) is added in Task 3.6, not here.

- [ ] **Step 9** — Rewrite `plugins/helium/src/index.ts` as the thin cordis adapter
      that reads the pinned env contract and supplies the two engine ports:

```ts
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import type { Context } from "@deepseek-ai/cordis";
import type {} from "@deepseek-ai/cordis-plugin-loader";
import {
  installModelSelection,
  type Agent,
  type ModelSelectionRef,
} from "@deepseek-ai/dsh-agent";
import type {} from "@deepseek-ai/dsh-agent-default-model";
import { createUserMessage } from "@deepseek-ai/dsh-llm";
import { SessionId, type SessionEvent } from "@deepseek-ai/dsh-session";
import type { JobSpec } from "@helium/core";
import { runClaude } from "./claude.js";
import { deliver, heartbeat } from "./delivery.js";
import { HeliumRuntime, type RuntimeConfig } from "./runtime.js";
import { registerToolkit } from "./toolkit.js";

export const name = "helium";
export const inject = ["agentDefaultModel", "agents", "sessions"];
export type Config = RuntimeConfig;

function finalText(events: readonly SessionEvent[], firstSeq: number): string {
  let text = "";
  for (const e of events) {
    if (e.seq < firstSeq || e.type !== "assistant/message") continue;
    const joined = e.data.message.content
      .filter((b) => b.type === "text")
      .map((b) => b.text)
      .join("");
    if (joined !== "") text = joined;
  }
  return text;
}

export function apply(ctx: Context, config: Config): void {
  registerToolkit(ctx, config);
  let agent: Agent | undefined;

  async function ensureAgent(): Promise<Agent> {
    if (agent) return agent;
    await ctx.get("loader")?.await();
    const selection = ctx.agentDefaultModel.currentSelection();
    const created = await ctx.agents.create({
      sessionId: SessionId(`helium-${randomUUID()}`),
      meta: { cwd: config.jobsDir },
      agentOptions: { provider: selection.provider, model: selection.model },
      setup: (agentCtx) => {
        const selected: ModelSelectionRef = {
          current: selection,
          assembled: undefined,
        };
        installModelSelection(agentCtx, selected);
      },
    });
    await created.agent.whenIdle();
    agent = created.agent;
    return agent;
  }

  const runtime = new HeliumRuntime({
    config,
    engines: {
      async triage(job: JobSpec, prompt: string) {
        const target = await ensureAgent();
        const firstSeq = target.session.seq;
        target.followup(
          createUserMessage({
            content: [{ type: "text", text: prompt }],
            source: { kind: "user" },
          }),
        );
        await target.whenIdle();
        await ctx.sessions.flush(target.session);
        const text = finalText(target.session.events, firstSeq);
        return text === ""
          ? { ok: false, error: "empty triage response" }
          : { ok: true, text };
      },
      async senior(job: JobSpec, prompt: string) {
        return runClaude({
          prompt,
          cwd: config.jobsDir,
          maxTurns: job.maxTurns.senior,
          timeoutMs: job.timeoutMs,
          allowedTools: job.tools,
          mcpConfigPath: config.mcpBin ? undefined : undefined,
          env: { HTTPS_PROXY: config.proxy, HTTP_PROXY: config.proxy },
        });
      },
    },
    delivery: {
      deliver: (job, ev, r) => deliver(job, ev, r),
      heartbeat: (row) => heartbeat(row),
    },
  });

  ctx.effect(() => {
    runtime.start();
    return () => runtime.stop();
  }, "helium.runtime()");
}
```

The `mcpConfigPath` line is a stub only until Step 10 replaces it.

- [ ] **Step 12** — Write `scripts/fixtures/argon-fixture.mjs` — a standalone
      mutable argon stand-in, runnable on laptop and mini:

```js
#!/usr/bin/env node
// Mutable argon stand-in. GET /api/rates/snapshot returns state.json;
// POST /__set with a JSON body replaces it. Prints the bound port on stdout.
import { createServer } from "node:http";
import { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";

const statePath = process.env.FIXTURE_STATE ?? "./fixture-state.json";
const port = Number(process.env.FIXTURE_PORT ?? 0);

const server = createServer((req, res) => {
  if (req.method === "POST" && req.url === "/__set") {
    let body = "";
    req.on("data", (c) => {
      body += c;
    });
    req.on("end", () => {
      writeFileSync(statePath, body, "utf8");
      res.writeHead(204).end();
    });
    return;
  }
  res.writeHead(200, { "content-type": "application/json" });
  res.end(readFileSync(statePath, "utf8"));
});
server.listen(port, "127.0.0.1", () => {
  const addr = server.address();
  console.log(
    JSON.stringify({
      port: typeof addr === "object" && addr ? addr.port : port,
    }),
  );
});
```

```ts
import {

enabled: true

    interval: 200ms

  triage: { engine: deepseek, model: deepseek-v4-flash }
  senior: { engine: claude-max }

`;
```

```ts
    await waitFor(() => readdirSync(join(stateRoot, "sensors")).length === 1, 3000);
    await fx.set({ regime: { state: "hawkish" }, direction: "up", confidence: 0.8 });
    await waitFor(() => mails.length === 1, 5000);              // 1: trigger + dispatch
    expect((mails[0] as { r: { verdict?: { severity: string } } }).r.verdict?.severity)
      .toBe("material");                                        // 2: verdict parsed
    expect(readFileSync(claudeLog, "utf8")).toContain("--max-turns"); // 3: claude ran
    const day = new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10);
    expect(readFileSync(join(stateRoot, "jsonl", `runs-${day}.jsonl`), "utf8"))
      .toContain('"tier":"senior"');                            // 4: runs ledger
    expect(readdirSync(join(stateRoot, "reports", "e2e-watch")).length).toBe(1); // 5: report
    const before = mails.length;
    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 800));
    expect(mails.length).toBe(before);                          // 6: unchanged poll is silent
    rt.stop();
    const rt2 = make();                                         // restart: baseline persisted
    rt2.start();
    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 800));
    expect(mails.length).toBe(before);
    rt2.stop();
  }, 20_000);
```

Add a local `waitFor(pred, ms)` helper that polls every 50 ms and throws on timeout.
Heartbeat rows are asserted separately by passing a recording `heartbeat` port and
expecting `≥ 3` rows after 800 ms at a 200 ms interval.

- [ ] **Step 4** — Replace the `prompt:` placeholder with this verbatim text
      (three parts: shared frame, triage contract, senior deliverable — one prompt
      serves both tiers because dispatch layers it under the ecosystem context and
      above the trigger payload):

```yaml
prompt: |
  ## PART A — WHAT THIS JOB WATCHES (both tiers read this)

  You are watching the US macro state that argon reconstructs: the policy-rate
  path, inflation prints, the term structure (short end vs long end), the dollar,
  and gold. A trigger fires when argon's own regime/direction/confidence view of
  that state changes, when a scheduled FOMC / CPI / NFP window opens, or on the
  weekday 17:00 America/New_York synthesis tick.

  The trigger payload in LAYER 3 gives you the previous and current values of the
  watched fields. Everything else you need you must fetch with the argon_api tool
  (read-only): /api/rates/snapshot, /api/macro/policy, /api/macro/inflation,
  /api/macro/rates, /api/macro/usd, /api/macro/gold, /api/gold/*, /api/regime.
  Never assume a number you have not read from a tool response. If a route is
  unreachable, say the state is unknown — a timeout is not evidence of a move.

  ## PART B — IF YOU ARE THE TRIAGE MODEL

  Decide only one thing: is this change material enough to spend a senior analyst
  run on it? Materiality means the change plausibly moves the rate-path thesis,
  not that a number moved. A confidence wobble inside a stable regime is noise.
  A regime flip, a direction reversal, a confidence break through a level, or a
  first policy datapoint inside a calendar window is material.

  Do not write analysis. Do not call tools. Answer from LAYER 3 alone, and emit
  exactly one JSON object as the last thing in your reply:

  {"escalate": true|false, "severity": "noise"|"minor"|"material"|"critical",
   "reason": "<= 200 chars"}

  ## PART C — IF YOU ARE THE SENIOR ANALYST

  You are the macro analyst for a single operator who trades US equity options and
  holds gold and large-cap tech exposure. Produce a markdown note answering, in
  this order and no other:

  1. **Rate path.** What does this change do to the expected policy path? Separate
     the short end (policy expectations, front of the curve) from the long end
     (term premium, growth/inflation expectations). Say which end moved and why.
  2. **Gold.** What does that rate-path read imply for gold — real rates, dollar,
     and the policy-credibility channel, in that order of weight.
  3. **Tech exposure.** What does it imply for long-duration equity exposure.
     Duration sensitivity, not sector storytelling.
  4. **Changed vs the standing thesis.** LAYER 2b holds the current thesis if one
     exists. State explicitly what is unchanged, what is amended, and what — if
     anything — would now falsify the thesis. If there is no standing thesis, say
     so and treat this as the initial statement.

  Then, and only if item 4 produced an amendment, call the `thesis_write` tool
  once with the complete new thesis (not a patch). The thesis must contain: the
  rate-path call, an explicit confidence, the two or three observations that would
  falsify it, and the date. It is capped at 64 KiB and every version is kept; the
  diff versus the previous version goes into the delivery email, so write it as a
  document a human will read side-by-side with its predecessor.

  Hard rules:
  - Narrative only. You never write numbers, scores or signals back into argon;
    argon owns quantitative promotion. Your output is prose plus the thesis file.
  - No trading instructions, no position sizing, no order suggestions.
  - Quiet is a valid outcome. If the honest answer is "this does not move the
    thesis", write four short paragraphs saying exactly that and skip thesis_write.
  - Cite the argon route you read each number from, inline, e.g. (/api/macro/rates).
```

### Task 3.3: Mini deployment (first, manual)

Put the harness on the mini by hand. Release automation comes in 3.5; doing it
manually once is what makes 3.5 writable. Everything before the plist install is
read-only against production.

**Files:**

- `scripts/prereq-check.sh` (new)
- `launchd/com.helium.dsh.plist.template` (new)
- `scripts/launchd/run-dsh.sh` (new)

**Interfaces:** `scripts/deploy-profile.sh --dsh-home <dir>` (Phase 2).

- [ ] **Step 1** — Write `scripts/prereq-check.sh` (runs on the mini, writes nothing):

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# helium mini prerequisites. Read-only: no installs, no writes, no secret values printed.
set -euo pipefail

fail=0
pass() { printf 'PASS  %-14s %s\n' "$1" "$2"; }
bad()  { printf 'FAIL  %-14s %s\n' "$1" "$2"; fail=1; }
warn() { printf 'WARN  %-14s %s\n' "$1" "$2"; }

if node_bin=$(command -v node 2>/dev/null); then
  major=$(node -p 'process.versions.node.split(".")[0]')
  if [ "$major" -ge 20 ]; then pass node "$node_bin $(node -v)"
  else bad node "$node_bin $(node -v) — need >= 20"; fi
else bad node "not on PATH (PATH=$PATH)"; fi

if pnpm_bin=$(command -v pnpm 2>/dev/null); then pass pnpm "$pnpm_bin $(pnpm -v)"
else bad pnpm "not on PATH"; fi

if claude_bin=$(command -v claude 2>/dev/null); then
  pass claude "$claude_bin $(claude --version 2>&1 | head -1)"
else bad claude "not on PATH"; fi

if nc -z -w 2 127.0.0.1 7897; then pass clash "127.0.0.1:7897 accepting"
else bad clash "127.0.0.1:7897 closed — start Clash Verge (mixed port)"; fi

tok="$HOME/.config/helium/claude-token.env"
if [ -f "$tok" ]; then
  mode=$(stat -f '%Lp' "$tok")
  if [ "$mode" = "600" ]; then pass token-file "$tok mode 600"
  else bad token-file "$tok mode $mode — run: chmod 600 $tok"; fi
else bad token-file "$tok missing"; fi

env_file="$HOME/.config/helium/helium.env"
if [ -f "$env_file" ]; then
  mode=$(stat -f '%Lp' "$env_file")
  if [ "$mode" = "600" ]; then pass env-file "$env_file mode 600"
  else bad env-file "$env_file mode $mode — run: chmod 600 $env_file"; fi
else bad env-file "$env_file missing (DEEPSEEK_API_KEY + SMTP)"; fi

sleep_val=$(pmset -g custom 2>/dev/null | awk '$1=="sleep"{print $2; exit}')
if [ "${sleep_val:-x}" = "0" ]; then pass pmset "system sleep disabled"
else warn pmset "sleep=${sleep_val:-unknown} — fix: sudo pmset -a sleep 0"; fi

code=$(curl -sS -m 5 -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' http://127.0.0.1:8400/api/health || true)
if [ "$code" = "200" ]; then pass argon "GET /api/health 200"
else bad argon "GET /api/health -> ${code:-no-response}"; fi

if launchctl print "gui/$(id -u)" >/dev/null 2>&1; then pass launchd-gui "gui/$(id -u) reachable"
else bad launchd-gui "gui/$(id -u) unreachable from this session — see Step 3"; fi

exit "$fail"
```

```bash
# token-age: mtime of the token file approximates the setup-token issue date (spec §4)

  age_days=$(( ( $(date +%s) - $(stat -f %m "$tok") ) / 86400 ))
  if [ "$age_days" -gt 330 ]; then
    echo "WARN token-age: ${age_days}d — 1-year token, re-run 'claude setup-token' soon"
  else
    echo "PASS token-age: ${age_days}d"
  fi
else
  echo "FAIL token-age: $tok missing"; fail=1
fi
```

- [ ] **Step 12** — Write `scripts/launchd/run-dsh.sh`. The plist must not contain
      secrets, so this wrapper — not launchd — sources `helium.env` and then `exec`s the
      real dsh bin from the release checkout:

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# launchd entry point for the helium dsh daemon. Sources secrets from the 0600 env
# file (never the plist), then execs the release's own dsh binary.
set -euo pipefail

release="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/../.." && pwd -P)"
env_file="${HELIUM_ENV_FILE:-/Users/moremeds/.config/helium/helium.env}"

if [ -r "$env_file" ]; then
  set -a
  # shellcheck disable=SC1090
  . "$env_file"
  set +a
else
  echo "run-dsh: env file $env_file unreadable — deepseek lane will fail" >&2
fi

dsh_bin="$release/node_modules/.bin/dsh"
[ -x "$dsh_bin" ] || { echo "run-dsh: $dsh_bin not executable" >&2; exit 78; }

exec "$dsh_bin" --profile helium web
```

- [ ] **Step 13** — Write `launchd/com.helium.dsh.plist.template`. `__NODE_BIN_DIR__`
      is the only token; Step 14 renders it from the real value printed by Step 2:

```xml
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8"?>
<!DOCTYPE plist PUBLIC "-//Apple//DTD PLIST 1.0//EN" "http://www.apple.com/DTDs/PropertyList-1.0.dtd">
<plist version="1.0">
<dict>
  <key>Label</key><string>com.helium.dsh</string>
  <key>ProgramArguments</key>
  <array>
    <string>/bin/bash</string>
    <string>/Users/moremeds/projects/helium-releases/current/scripts/launchd/run-dsh.sh</string>
  </array>
  <key>WorkingDirectory</key><string>/Users/moremeds/projects/helium-releases/current</string>
  <key>RunAtLoad</key><true/>
  <key>KeepAlive</key><true/>
  <key>ThrottleInterval</key><integer>10</integer>
  <key>ExitTimeOut</key><integer>30</integer>
  <key>ProcessType</key><string>Standard</string>
```

### Task 3.4: External dead-man agent

The harness cannot report its own death. A second, deliberately tiny launchd agent
watches the heartbeat file and emails when it goes stale — independent of the dsh
process, its plugins, and its delivery path.

**Files:**

- `scripts/deadman/check-heartbeat.sh` (new)
- `scripts/deadman/send-alert.mjs` (new)
- `launchd/com.helium.deadman.plist.template` (new)
- `scripts/deadman/check-heartbeat.test.sh` (new — local drill harness)

**Interfaces:** SMTP variable names must match what Phase 2's
`plugins/helium/src/delivery.ts` reads. Read that file first; the names below are
the pinned contract — if delivery used different ones, change **this script**, not
delivery.

- [ ] **Step 1** — Read `plugins/helium/src/delivery.ts` and confirm the SMTP env
      names. Pinned contract: `SMTP_HOST`, `SMTP_PORT`,
      `SMTP_SECURE`, `SMTP_USER`, `SMTP_PASS`, `SMTP_FROM`,
      `HELIUM_EMAIL_TO`. Record any deviation in the commit message.

- [ ] **Step 2** — Write `scripts/deadman/send-alert.mjs`:

```js
#!/usr/bin/env node
// Minimal alert mailer. Reads SMTP config from a 0600 env file; never prints
// a secret value. Exit: 0 sent, 2 config error, 3 send failed.
import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import nodemailer from "nodemailer";

const args = new Map();
for (let i = 2; i < process.argv.length; i += 2)
  args.set(process.argv[i], process.argv[i + 1]);
const envFile =
  args.get("--env-file") ?? "/Users/moremeds/.config/helium/helium.env";
const subject = args.get("--subject") ?? "helium alert";
const body = args.get("--body-file")
  ? readFileSync(args.get("--body-file"), "utf8")
  : (args.get("--body") ?? "");

const cfg = {};
for (const line of readFileSync(envFile, "utf8").split("\n")) {
  const m = /^\s*(?:export\s+)?([A-Z0-9_]+)\s*=\s*(.*)$/.exec(line);
  if (!m) continue;
  cfg[m[1]] = m[2].replace(/^(['"])(.*)\1$/, "$2").trim();
}
const need = [
  "SMTP_HOST",
  "SMTP_PORT",
  "SMTP_USER",
  "SMTP_PASS",
  "SMTP_FROM",
  "HELIUM_EMAIL_TO",
];
const missing = need.filter((k) => !cfg[k]);
if (missing.length > 0) {
  console.log(
    JSON.stringify({ ok: false, error: "missing config keys", missing }),
  );
  process.exit(2);
}
try {
  const info = await nodemailer
    .createTransport({
      host: cfg.SMTP_HOST,
      port: Number(cfg.SMTP_PORT),
      secure:
        cfg.SMTP_SECURE === "true" || Number(cfg.SMTP_PORT) === 465,
      auth: { user: cfg.SMTP_USER, pass: cfg.SMTP_PASS },
    })
    .sendMail({
      from: cfg.SMTP_FROM,
      to: cfg.HELIUM_EMAIL_TO,
      subject,
      text: body,
    });
  console.log(
    JSON.stringify({
      ok: true,
      messageId: info.messageId,
      accepted: info.accepted.length,
    }),
  );
} catch (e) {
  console.log(
    JSON.stringify({
      ok: false,
      error: String(e && e.message ? e.message : e),
    }),
  );
  process.exit(3);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3** — Write `scripts/deadman/check-heartbeat.sh`:

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# External liveness check for the helium daemon. Runs from its own launchd agent
# every 30 minutes. Emails once per staleness episode, re-alerting at most every 6h.
# Exit: 0 fresh, 10 stale+alerted, 11 stale+suppressed, 12 stale+alert failed, 2 config.
set -euo pipefail

STATE_ROOT="${HELIUM_STATE_ROOT:-/Users/moremeds/.helium/state}"
NODE_BIN="${HELIUM_NODE_BIN:-node}"
ENV_FILE="${HELIUM_ENV_FILE:-/Users/moremeds/.config/helium/helium.env}"
STALE_S="${HELIUM_DEADMAN_STALE_S:-600}"
REALERT_S="${HELIUM_DEADMAN_REALERT_S:-21600}"
script_dir="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd -P)"
ALERT_CMD="${HELIUM_DEADMAN_ALERT_CMD:-$NODE_BIN $script_dir/send-alert.mjs}"

command -v "${NODE_BIN%% *}" >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo "no node at $NODE_BIN" >&2; exit 2; }

sentinel_dir="$STATE_ROOT/deadman"
sentinel="$sentinel_dir/alerted-at"
mkdir -p "$sentinel_dir"

newest=$(find "$STATE_ROOT/jsonl" -name 'heartbeat-*.jsonl' -type f 2>/dev/null \
  | sort | tail -1)
now=$(date -u +%s)

if [ -z "$newest" ]; then
  last_epoch=0
  last_iso="(no heartbeat file)"
else
  last_iso=$("$NODE_BIN" -e '
    const {readFileSync}=require("node:fs");
    const lines=readFileSync(process.argv[1],"utf8").trim().split("\n").filter(Boolean);
    for(let i=lines.length-1;i>=0;i--){
      try{const ts=JSON.parse(lines[i]).ts; if(ts){process.stdout.write(ts);return;}}catch{}
    }
    process.stdout.write("");' "$newest")
  if [ -z "$last_iso" ]; then last_epoch=0; last_iso="(unparseable)"
  else last_epoch=$("$NODE_BIN" -e \
    'process.stdout.write(String(Math.floor(Date.parse(process.argv[1])/1000)||0))' "$last_iso")
  fi
fi

age=$(( now - last_epoch ))
if [ "$last_epoch" -gt 0 ] && [ "$age" -lt "$STALE_S" ]; then
  rm -f "$sentinel"
  echo "fresh: last heartbeat ${age}s ago ($last_iso)"
  exit 0
fi

if [ -f "$sentinel" ]; then
  prev=$(cat "$sentinel")
  if [ $(( now - prev )) -lt "$REALERT_S" ]; then
    echo "stale (${age}s) but alerted $(( now - prev ))s ago — suppressed"
    exit 11
  fi
fi

body_file=$(mktemp -t helium-deadman)
trap 'rm -f "$body_file"' EXIT
{
  echo "helium heartbeat is stale."
  echo "last heartbeat: $last_iso (${age}s ago; threshold ${STALE_S}s)"
  echo "heartbeat file: ${newest:-none}"
  echo "checked at:     $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)"
  echo
  echo "Check: ssh macmini 'launchctl print gui/\$(id -u)/com.helium.dsh'"
  echo "Logs:  /Users/moremeds/.helium/logs/dsh.err.log"
} > "$body_file"

if $ALERT_CMD --env-file "$ENV_FILE" \
     --subject "[helium] heartbeat stale since $last_iso" \
     --body-file "$body_file"; then
  echo "$now" > "$sentinel"
  echo "stale (${age}s) — alert sent"
  exit 10
fi
echo "stale (${age}s) — alert FAILED" >&2
exit 12
```

- [ ] **Step 4** — Write `scripts/deadman/check-heartbeat.test.sh` — a local drill
      against a throwaway state dir, with a fake mailer so nothing is sent:

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Local drill for check-heartbeat.sh. No network, no real SMTP.
set -euo pipefail
here="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd -P)"
tmp=$(mktemp -d -t helium-deadman-test)
trap 'rm -rf "$tmp"' EXIT
mkdir -p "$tmp/state/jsonl"
cat > "$tmp/fake-mailer" <<'EOS'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
printf '{"ok":true,"fake":true}\n'
printf '%s\n' "$*" >> "$FAKE_MAIL_LOG"
EOS
chmod +x "$tmp/fake-mailer"
export FAKE_MAIL_LOG="$tmp/mail.log"
export HELIUM_STATE_ROOT="$tmp/state" HELIUM_DEADMAN_ALERT_CMD="$tmp/fake-mailer"
export HELIUM_ENV_FILE="$tmp/helium.env"; : > "$tmp/helium.env"
run() { set +e; bash "$here/check-heartbeat.sh" >/dev/null 2>&1; echo $?; set -e; }

echo "case 1: no heartbeat file at all -> 10"
[ "$(run)" = 10 ] || { echo FAIL-1; exit 1; }
echo "case 2: immediately again -> 11 (6h dedup)"
[ "$(run)" = 11 ] || { echo FAIL-2; exit 1; }
echo "case 3: fresh heartbeat -> 0 and sentinel cleared"
printf '{"ts":"%s","job":"macro-watch","status":"ok"}\n' \
  "$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" \
  > "$tmp/state/jsonl/heartbeat-$(date -u +%F).jsonl"
[ "$(run)" = 0 ] || { echo FAIL-3; exit 1; }
[ ! -f "$tmp/state/deadman/alerted-at" ] || { echo FAIL-3b; exit 1; }
echo "case 4: 20-minute-old heartbeat -> 10"
printf '{"ts":"%s","job":"macro-watch","status":"ok"}\n' \
  "$(date -u -v-20M +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)" \
  > "$tmp/state/jsonl/heartbeat-$(date -u +%F).jsonl"
[ "$(run)" = 10 ] || { echo FAIL-4; exit 1; }
echo "mail log:"; cat "$FAKE_MAIL_LOG"
echo "ALL PASS"
```


`HELIUM_NODE_BIN` is set explicitly because `send-alert.mjs` and the timestamp
parsing must not depend on shell PATH resolution inside a launchd job. Residual
risk, recorded deliberately: the deadman resolves `nodemailer` out of `current`, so
a release whose `node_modules` is broken also breaks the alerter — Task 3.5's
pre-flip contract smoke is what keeps that from shipping.

### Task 3.5: Release scripts and release drill (AC#6)

Replace the manual deploy with three scripts and prove the full cut → deploy →
rollback loop against the running mini.

**Deploy mechanism (decided — do not re-litigate):** the mini keeps one ordinary
git clone at `/Users/moremeds/projects/helium` as the _source of tags only_. A
release is materialised with `git archive` piped into `tar -x`, so the release
directory is an immutable tree with no `.git`, no worktree registration and no way
to accidentally check something out over a running release. The tarball path also
means the release directory can never be mutated by a later `git` operation.

**Files:** `scripts/release/cut.sh`, `scripts/release/deploy.sh`,
`scripts/release/rollback.sh`, `scripts/release/inflight.mjs`,
`scripts/deploy-profile.sh` (edit: add `--plugin-dir`), `CHANGELOG.md` (new).

**Preamble to fill before writing `deploy.sh`:** paste the Task 3.3 Step 10 answer
here (symlink vs copy). It decides one line of `deploy.sh`: if the profile's
`dsh-plugin-helium` is a _symlink into the release dir_, the profile re-deploy on
rollback is optional; if it is a _copy_, the re-deploy is mandatory on every flip.
`deploy.sh` below does it unconditionally, which is correct either way.

- [ ] **Step 1** — Extend `scripts/deploy-profile.sh` with an optional
      `--plugin-dir <abs-path>` (default: the repo-relative `plugins/helium` next to the
      script). Add to its argument loop:

```bash
    --plugin-dir) plugin_dir="$2"; shift 2 ;;
```

and default it before the loop:

```bash
plugin_dir="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/../plugins/helium" && pwd -P)"
```

then use `"$plugin_dir"` in the `dsh plugin add file:` invocation. Nothing else
changes; `--dsh-home` keeps its Phase 2 meaning.

not logged in, the documented alternative is a read-only deploy key:
`ssh macmini 'ssh-keygen -t ed25519 -N "" -f ~/.ssh/helium_deploy'`, then
`gh repo deploy-key add ~/.ssh/helium_deploy.pub --repo moremeds/helium` from the

mini's `~/.ssh/config`. Do not proceed until one of the two works.

Expected: a branch name (`master` or `dev`) and no error.

- [ ] **Step 4** — Write `scripts/release/inflight.mjs` (used by the drain gate):

```js
#!/usr/bin/env node
// Prints the number of runs that have a run_started row with no terminal row.
import { readFileSync, readdirSync } from "node:fs";
import { join } from "node:path";

const dir = join(
  process.env.HELIUM_STATE_ROOT ?? "/Users/moremeds/.helium/state",
  "jsonl",
);
const files = readdirSync(dir)
  .filter((f) => f.startsWith("runs-"))
  .sort()
  .slice(-2);
const open = new Set();
for (const f of files) {
  for (const line of readFileSync(join(dir, f), "utf8")
    .split("\n")
    .filter(Boolean)) {
    let r;
    try {
      r = JSON.parse(line);
    } catch {
      continue;
    }
    if (!r.runId) continue;
    if (r.phase === "run_started") open.add(r.runId);
    else open.delete(r.runId);
  }
}
console.log(open.size);
```

- [ ] **Step 5** — Write `scripts/release/cut.sh` (laptop only):

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Cut a helium release tag. Laptop only. Refuses a dirty tree.
# usage: cut.sh vX.Y.Z [--allow-branch <name>]
set -euo pipefail

version="${1:-}"
allow_branch=""
shift || true
while [ $# -gt 0 ]; do
  case "$1" in
    --allow-branch) allow_branch="$2"; shift 2 ;;
    *) echo "unknown argument: $1" >&2; exit 64 ;;
  esac
done

[[ "$version" =~ ^v[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$ ]] || { echo "usage: cut.sh vX.Y.Z" >&2; exit 64; }
repo_root="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"
cd "$repo_root"

[ -z "$(git status --porcelain)" ] || { echo "refusing: working tree is dirty" >&2; exit 65; }
branch="$(git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD)"
if [ "$branch" != "master" ] && [ "$branch" != "$allow_branch" ]; then
  echo "refusing: on '$branch'; cut from master or pass --allow-branch $branch" >&2
  exit 65
fi
git fetch origin --tags --quiet
git rev-parse -q --verify "refs/tags/$version" >/dev/null && {
  echo "refusing: tag $version already exists" >&2; exit 65; }

node -e '
  const {readFileSync,writeFileSync}=require("node:fs");
  const p=JSON.parse(readFileSync("package.json","utf8"));
  p.version=process.argv[1].slice(1);
  writeFileSync("package.json",JSON.stringify(p,null,2)+"\n");' "$version"

prev="$(git describe --tags --abbrev=0 2>/dev/null || true)"
range="${prev:+$prev..}HEAD"
entry="$(mktemp -t helium-changelog)"
{
  echo "## $version — $(date -u +%Y-%m-%d)"
  echo
  git log --no-merges --pretty='- %s' "$range"
  echo
} > "$entry"
"${EDITOR:-vi}" "$entry"
touch CHANGELOG.md
cat "$entry" CHANGELOG.md > CHANGELOG.md.new && mv CHANGELOG.md.new CHANGELOG.md
rm -f "$entry"

git add package.json CHANGELOG.md
git commit -m "release: $version"
git tag -a "$version" -m "helium $version"
git push origin "HEAD:$branch" && git push origin "$version"
echo "cut $version on $branch"
```

      re-exec itself on the mini over stdin so there is exactly one file):

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Deploy a tagged helium release to the mini. Run from the laptop:
#   scripts/release/deploy.sh v0.1.0
set -euo pipefail

VERSION="${1:-}"
[[ "$VERSION" =~ ^v[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$ ]] || { echo "usage: deploy.sh vX.Y.Z" >&2; exit 64; }

RELEASES=/Users/moremeds/projects/helium-releases
SRC=/Users/moremeds/projects/helium
DSH_HOME_DIR=/Users/moremeds/.helium/dsh-home
STATE_ROOT=/Users/moremeds/.helium/state
KEEP=5

if [ "${HELIUM_REMOTE:-0}" != "1" ]; then
  ssh macmini "export PATH=\"\$HOME/.local/bin:\$PATH\"; HELIUM_REMOTE=1 bash -s -- $VERSION" < "$0"
  exit $?
fi
# ---- everything below runs ON the mini ----
say() { printf '[deploy %s] %s\n' "$VERSION" "$*"; }
```

- [ ] **Step 7** — `deploy.sh` part 2 (materialise the immutable release):

```bash
say "fetching tags"
git -C "$SRC" fetch --tags --prune --quiet
git -C "$SRC" rev-parse -q --verify "refs/tags/$VERSION^{commit}" >/dev/null \
  || { echo "tag $VERSION not found in $SRC" >&2; exit 65; }

DEST="$RELEASES/$VERSION"
if [ -d "$DEST" ]; then
  say "release dir already exists — reusing (immutable by construction)"
else
  mkdir -p "$DEST.partial"
  git -C "$SRC" archive "$VERSION" | tar -x -C "$DEST.partial"
  mv "$DEST.partial" "$DEST"
fi
say "installing"
( cd "$DEST" && pnpm install --frozen-lockfile && pnpm build )
installed=$(node -p "require('$DEST/node_modules/@deepseek-ai/dsh/package.json').version")
[ "$installed" = "0.1.1-rc.2" ] || { echo "dsh pin drift: $installed" >&2; exit 66; }
say "dsh pin ok: $installed"
```

- [ ] **Step 8** — `deploy.sh` part 3 (pre-flip smoke, profile deploy, drain):

```bash
say "contract smoke (one live deepseek-v4-flash call)"
( cd "$DEST" && set -a && . /Users/moremeds/.config/helium/helium.env && set +a && \
  HELIUM_LIVE=1 DSH_HOME="$(mktemp -d -t helium-smoke)" pnpm -F @helium/contracts test ) \
  || { echo "contract smoke FAILED — aborting before flip" >&2; exit 67; }

say "deploying profile (plugin resolved from $DEST)"
bash "$DEST/scripts/deploy-profile.sh" --dsh-home "$DSH_HOME_DIR" \
  --plugin-dir "$DEST/plugins/helium"

say "draining in-flight dispatches (max 120s)"
end=$((SECONDS + 120))
while [ $SECONDS -lt $end ]; do
  n=$(HELIUM_STATE_ROOT="$STATE_ROOT" node "$DEST/scripts/release/inflight.mjs")
  [ "$n" = "0" ] && break
  say "  $n in flight"
  sleep 5
done
n=$(HELIUM_STATE_ROOT="$STATE_ROOT" node "$DEST/scripts/release/inflight.mjs")
[ "$n" = "0" ] || say "WARNING: proceeding with $n dispatch(es) still in flight"
```

- [ ] **Step 9** — `deploy.sh` part 4 (atomic flip + verified health window +
      automatic flip-back). `mv -fh` is the macOS-correct idiom: `-h` stops `mv` from
      following the existing `current` symlink into its target directory, so the rename
      is atomic:

```bash
flip_to() {
  local target="$1" tmp="$RELEASES/.current.$$"
  ln -sfn "$target" "$tmp"
  mv -fh "$tmp" "$RELEASES/current"
  local got; got="$(readlink "$RELEASES/current")"
  [ "$got" = "$target" ] || { echo "flip verification failed: current -> $got" >&2; return 1; }
}
prev_target="$(readlink "$RELEASES/current" || true)"
[ -n "$prev_target" ] && { ln -sfn "$prev_target" "$RELEASES/.previous.$$"; \
  mv -fh "$RELEASES/.previous.$$" "$RELEASES/previous"; }

flip_start=$(date -u +%s)
flip_to "$DEST"
say "flipped current -> $VERSION; restarting daemon"
launchctl kickstart -k "gui/$(id -u)/com.helium.dsh"

say "post-flip health window (2 heartbeat intervals)"
ok=0; end=$((SECONDS + 180))
while [ $SECONDS -lt $end ]; do
  sleep 15
  fresh=$(HELIUM_STATE_ROOT="$STATE_ROOT" node -e '
    const {readFileSync,readdirSync}=require("node:fs");const {join}=require("node:path");
    const d=join(process.env.HELIUM_STATE_ROOT,"jsonl");
    const f=readdirSync(d).filter(x=>x.startsWith("heartbeat-")).sort().pop();
    const since=Number(process.argv[1])*1000;
    const n=readFileSync(join(d,f),"utf8").split("\n").filter(Boolean)
      .filter(l=>{try{return Date.parse(JSON.parse(l).ts)>since;}catch{return false;}}).length;
    console.log(n);' "$flip_start")
  say "  heartbeat rows since flip: $fresh"
  [ "$fresh" -ge 2 ] && { ok=1; break; }
done
if [ "$ok" != "1" ]; then
  say "HEALTH WINDOW FAILED — flipping back to $prev_target"
  flip_to "$prev_target"
  bash "$prev_target/scripts/deploy-profile.sh" --dsh-home "$DSH_HOME_DIR" \
    --plugin-dir "$prev_target/plugins/helium"
  launchctl kickstart -k "gui/$(id -u)/com.helium.dsh"
  exit 68
fi
```

- [ ] **Step 10** — `deploy.sh` part 5 (prune, keeping the last 5 plus whatever
      `current` and `previous` point at):

```bash
say "pruning old releases (keep $KEEP)"
keep_now="$(basename "$(readlink "$RELEASES/current")")"
keep_prev="$(basename "$(readlink "$RELEASES/previous" 2>/dev/null || echo "")")"
ls -1dt "$RELEASES"/v*/ 2>/dev/null | tail -n +$((KEEP + 1)) | while read -r old; do
  base="$(basename "$old")"
  [ "$base" = "$keep_now" ] && continue
  [ "$base" = "$keep_prev" ] && continue
  say "  removing $base"
  rm -rf "$old"
done
say "DEPLOY OK: $VERSION"
```

- [ ] **Step 11** — Write `scripts/release/rollback.sh` (same self-ssh trick;
      measures and prints its own wall time because AC#6 is a stopwatch criterion):

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Roll back to the previous helium release. Run from the laptop: rollback.sh
set -euo pipefail
RELEASES=/Users/moremeds/projects/helium-releases
DSH_HOME_DIR=/Users/moremeds/.helium/dsh-home

if [ "${HELIUM_REMOTE:-0}" != "1" ]; then
  ssh macmini 'export PATH="$HOME/.local/bin:$PATH"; HELIUM_REMOTE=1 bash -s' < "$0"
  exit $?
fi
started=$(date -u +%s)
target="$(readlink "$RELEASES/previous")" || { echo "no previous release" >&2; exit 65; }
current="$(readlink "$RELEASES/current")"
[ -d "$target" ] || { echo "previous release $target missing" >&2; exit 65; }
echo "[rollback] $current -> $target"

tmp="$RELEASES/.current.$$"; ln -sfn "$target" "$tmp"; mv -fh "$tmp" "$RELEASES/current"
[ "$(readlink "$RELEASES/current")" = "$target" ] || { echo "flip failed" >&2; exit 66; }
tmp="$RELEASES/.previous.$$"; ln -sfn "$current" "$tmp"; mv -fh "$tmp" "$RELEASES/previous"

bash "$target/scripts/deploy-profile.sh" --dsh-home "$DSH_HOME_DIR" \
  --plugin-dir "$target/plugins/helium"
launchctl kickstart -k "gui/$(id -u)/com.helium.dsh"

end=$((SECONDS + 90)); ok=0
while [ $SECONDS -lt $end ]; do
  sleep 10
  pid=$(launchctl print "gui/$(id -u)/com.helium.dsh" 2>/dev/null | awk '/pid =/{print $3}')
  [ -n "${pid:-}" ] && { ok=1; break; }
done
echo "[rollback] running pid=${pid:-none} elapsed=$(( $(date -u +%s) - started ))s"
[ "$ok" = "1" ] || exit 67
```
